\displaystyle+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{|\eta_{1}|^{l_{1}}|\eta_{2}|^{l_{2}-1}}\frac{\sqrt{\phi}}{p|\eta_{2}|}\Psi(z_{2})\right).

Similarly, for
(\mathsf{A}_{i})_{3}
, using Theorem
5.5
, we see that

\displaystyle(\mathsf{A}_{i})_{3}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{\sigma_{i}}{{\sqrt{pn}}}\mathbb{E}\left\{((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}-1}(1-\mathbb{E})[({{R_{1}}(z_{2})}Y)_{ij}({{R_{1}}(z_{2})}Y)_{ij}]\right\}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\sigma_{i}}{{\sqrt{pn}}}\mathbb{E}\left[((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{1}}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}-1}(1-\mathbb{E})\left(R_{1}(z_{2})YY^{*}R_{1}(z_{2})\right)_{ii}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{|\eta_{1}|^{l_{1}}|\eta_{2}|^{l_{2}-1}}\frac{\sqrt{\phi}}{p|\eta_{2}|}\Psi(z_{2})\right).

Plugging the above estimates back into (
6.3
), we get

\displaystyle z_{2}\mathbb{E}\left\{(1-\mathbb{E})[{((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}-1}}]({R_{1}}(z_{2}))_{ii}\right\}

\displaystyle=(\mathsf{A}_{i})_{1}+(\mathsf{A}_{i})_{2}+(\mathsf{A}_{i})_{3}+\mathsf{B}_{i}+\mathsf{L}_{i}

\displaystyle=(\phi^{-1/2}\sigma_{i}-z_{2}\phi^{-1/2}\sigma_{i}m_{p}(z_{2})-\phi^{1/2}\sigma_{i})\mathbb{E}\left\{((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}-1}({{R_{1}}(z_{2})})_{ii}\right\}

\displaystyle+\frac{\phi^{1/2}\sigma_{i}}{p(1+\phi^{-1/2}m(z_{2})\sigma_{i})}\mathbb{E}\left\{((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}}\right\}

\displaystyle+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{|\eta_{1}|^{l_{1}}|\eta_{2}|^{l_{2}-1}}\frac{\sqrt{\phi}}{p|\eta_{2}|}\Psi(z_{2})\right)+\mathsf{B}_{i}+\mathsf{L}_{i}.

With straightforward calculations, we can further obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[{((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}}}]

(6.7)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left\{(1-\mathbb{E})[{((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}-1}}]\operatorname{Tr}({R_{1}}(z_{2}))\right\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{1}{z_{2}(1+\sigma_{i}\phi^{-1/2}m(z_{2}))}\left[\frac{\phi^{1/2}\sigma_{i}}{p(1+\phi^{-1/2}m(z_{2})\sigma_{i})}\mathbb{E}\left\{((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}}\right\}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{|\eta_{1}|^{l_{1}}|\eta_{2}|^{l_{2}-1}}\frac{\sqrt{\phi}}{p|\eta_{2}|}\Psi(z_{2})\right)+\mathsf{B}_{i}+\mathsf{L}_{i}\right].

Then we proceed to the calculation of
\mathsf{B}_{i}.
Again using (
6.4
)–(
6.6
), we have that

\displaystyle\mathsf{B}_{i}=

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{\sigma_{i}}}{\sqrt{np}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left\{\frac{\partial\left[(1-\mathbb{E}){((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}-1}}\right]}{\partial x_{ij}}({{R_{1}}(z_{2})}Y)_{ij}\right\}

(6.8)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\frac{\sigma_{i}}{\sqrt{np}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[l_{1}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}-1}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}-1}({R_{1}}(z_{2})Y)_{ij}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}z_{1}}({R_{1}}(z_{1})Y)_{ij}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+(l_{2}-1)((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}-2}({R_{1}}(z_{2})Y)_{ij}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}z_{2}}({R_{1}}(z_{2})Y)_{ij}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\frac{\sigma_{i}}{\sqrt{np}}\mathbb{E}\left[l_{1}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}-1}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}-1}\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{1}}({R_{1}}(z_{1})YY^{*}{{R_{1}}(z_{2})})\right)_{ii}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+(l_{2}-1)((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}-2}\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{2}}\frac{1}{2}({R_{1}}(z_{2})YY^{*}{{R_{1}}(z_{2})})\right)_{ii}\right]

\displaystyle:=(\mathsf{B}_{i})_{1}+(\mathsf{B}_{i})_{2}.

For the first term, we have that

\displaystyle(\mathsf{B}_{i})_{1}

\displaystyle=-\frac{2\sigma_{i}}{\sqrt{np}}\mathbb{E}\left[l_{1}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}-1}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}-1}\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{1}}({R_{1}}(z_{1})YY^{*}{{R_{1}}(z_{2})})\right)_{ii}\right]

\displaystyle=-\frac{2\sigma_{i}}{{\sqrt{pn}}}\mathbb{E}\left[l_{1}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}-1}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}-1}\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{1}}\left({R_{1}}\left(z_{1}\right)Q{R_{1}}\left(z_{2}\right)\right)_{ii}\right]

\displaystyle=-\frac{2\sigma_{i}}{{\sqrt{pn}}}\mathbb{E}\left[l_{1}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}-1}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}-1}\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{1}}\left(z_{1}{R_{1}}\left(z_{1}\right){R_{1}}\left(z_{2}\right)\right)_{ii}\right]

\displaystyle=-\frac{2\sigma_{i}}{{\sqrt{pn}}}\mathbb{E}\left[l_{1}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}-1}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}-1}\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{1}}\left(z_{1}\frac{{R_{1}}\left(z_{1}\right)-{R_{1}}\left(z_{2}\right)}{z_{1}-z_{2}}\right)_{ii}\right]

\displaystyle=-\frac{2}{{\sqrt{pn}}}\mathbb{E}\left[l_{1}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}-1}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}-1}\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{1}}\left(\frac{z_{1}\sigma_{i}(\mathfrak{g}_{i}(z_{1})-\mathfrak{g_{i}}(z_{2}))}{z_{1}-z_{2}}\right)\right]+\mathcal{E}_{B,1}(i),

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{B,1}(i):=-\frac{2\sigma_{i}}{{\sqrt{pn}}}\mathbb{E}\left[l_{1}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}-1}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}-1}\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{1}}\left(z_{1}\frac{({R_{1}}\left(z_{1}\right))_{ii}-\mathfrak{g}_{i}(z_{1})-({R_{1}}\left(z_{2}\right))_{ii}+\mathfrak{g}_{i}(z_{2})}{z_{1}-z_{2}}\right)\right],

\mathfrak{g}_{i}(z):=-\frac{1}{z(1+\phi^{-1/2}m(z)\sigma_{i})}.

(6.9)

From (
6.7
) we can see that
\sum_{i=1}^{p}\mathfrak{g}_{i}(z_{2})\mathcal{E}_{B,1}(i)
is the error term we need to control. Let
\eta_{i}=\operatorname{Im}z_{i},\ i=1,2.
On the one hand,
|z_{1}-z_{2}|\succ|\eta_{2}|
, using the fact that
z_{1}\asymp\sqrt{\phi}
,
\mathfrak{g}_{i}(z_{2})\asymp\phi^{-1/2}
and
\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{1}}\left(\cdot\right)\asymp|\eta_{1}|^{-1}
, we can use the fluctuation averaging argument as in the proof of (
5.17
) (c.f. Lemma
A.9
) to obtain that

\sum_{i}\mathfrak{g}_{i}(z_{2})\sigma_{i}((R_{1})_{ii}(z)-\mathfrak{g}_{i}(z))=\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(\frac{\phi^{-1/2}}{|\operatorname{Im}(z)|}\right).

This further results in

\sum_{i=1}^{p}\mathfrak{g}_{i}(z_{2})\mathcal{E}_{B,1}(i)=\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{pn}}\frac{1}{|\eta_{1}|^{l_{1}-1}|\eta_{2}|^{l_{2}-1}}\frac{1}{|\eta_{1}|}\frac{1}{|\eta_{2}|}\left(\frac{1}{|\eta_{1}|}+\frac{1}{|\eta_{2}|}\right)\right)=\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{|\eta_{1}|^{l_{1}}|\eta_{2}|^{l_{2}}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{pn}}\left(\frac{1}{|\eta_{1}|}+\frac{1}{|\eta_{2}|}\right)\right).

On the other hand, when
z_{1}
and
z_{2}
are close, without loss of generality, we assume that
\operatorname{Im}z_{1}\succ|\eta_{2}|
. Denote the contour
\Gamma=\partial\mathsf{B}_{c|\eta_{2}|}(z_{1})\cup\partial\mathsf{B}_{c|\eta_{2}|}(z_{2})
for some constant
c>0
, where choose
c>0
small enough so that
\Gamma\subset\mathbb{C}_{+}
and
\min_{\xi\in\Gamma}\operatorname{Im}\xi\succ|\eta_{2}|
. Using Theorem
5.5
, we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{p}\sigma_{i}\mathfrak{g}_{i}(R_{1}(z_{1})R_{1}(z_{2}))_{ii}=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{p}\sigma_{i}\frac{(R_{1}(z_{1})-R_{1}(z_{2}))_{ii}}{(z_{1}-z_{2})}=\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\sigma_{i}\int_{\Gamma}\frac{(R_{1}(\xi))_{ii}}{(\xi-z_{1})(\xi-z_{2})}\mathrm{~d}\xi

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\int_{\Gamma}\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{p}\sigma_{i}\left(\Pi(\xi)\right)_{ii}+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{|\eta_{2}|}\right)}{(\xi-z_{1})(\xi-z_{2})}\mathrm{~d}\xi=\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{p}\sigma_{i}(\mathfrak{g}_{i}(z_{1})-\mathfrak{g_{i}}(z_{2}))}{z_{1}-z_{2}}+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{|\eta_{2}|^{2}}\right),

which implies that
\sum_{i=1}^{p}\mathfrak{g}_{i}(z)\mathcal{E}_{B,1}(i)=\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{|\eta_{1}|^{l_{1}-1}|\eta_{2}|^{l_{2}-1}}\frac{1}{|\eta_{1}|}\frac{1}{\sqrt{pn}(|\eta_{2}|)^{2}}\right)=\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{|\eta_{1}|^{l_{1}}|\eta_{2}|^{l_{2}}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{pn}|\eta_{2}|}\right).
In summary, we always have that

\sum_{i=1}^{p}\mathfrak{g}_{i}(z_{2})\mathcal{E}_{B,1}(i)=\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{|\eta_{1}|^{l_{1}}|\eta_{2}|^{l_{2}}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{pn}}\left(\frac{1}{|\eta_{1}|}+\frac{1}{|\eta_{2}|}\right)\right).

(6.10)

Similarly, we can handle
(\mathsf{B}_{i})_{2}
in (
6.8
) as follows

\displaystyle(\mathsf{B}_{i})_{2}=

\displaystyle-\frac{2\sigma_{i}}{\sqrt{np}}\mathbb{E}\left[(l_{2}-1)((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}-2}\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{2}}\frac{1}{2}({R_{1}}(z_{2})YY^{*}{{R_{1}}(z_{2})})\right)_{ii}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{2\sigma_{i}}{\sqrt{np}}\mathbb{E}\left[(l_{2}-1)((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}-2}\left(\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{2}}({R_{1}}(z_{2})+z_{2}({R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{2})\right)_{ii}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{2\sigma_{i}}{\sqrt{np}}\mathbb{E}\left[(l_{2}-1)((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}-2}\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{2}}\left((\mathfrak{g}_{i}(z_{2})+z_{2}\mathfrak{g}_{i}^{\prime}(z_{2}))\right)\right]+\mathcal{E}_{B,2}(i),

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{B,2}(i):=-\frac{2\sigma_{i}}{{\sqrt{pn}}}\mathbb{E}\left[l_{1}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}-1}\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{2}}\Big(({R_{1}}\left(z_{2}\right))_{ii}-\mathfrak{g}_{i}(z_{2})+z_{2}(({R_{1}}\left(z_{2}\right))_{ii}-\mathfrak{g}_{i}(z_{2}))^{\prime}\Big)\right].

For the error term, by a discussion similar to (
6.10
), we have that

\sum_{i=1}^{p}\mathfrak{g}_{i}(z_{2})\mathcal{E}_{B,2}(i)=\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{|\eta_{1}|^{l_{1}}|\eta_{2}|^{l_{2}-2}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{pn}}\frac{1}{|\eta_{2}|}\frac{1}{|\eta_{2}|^{2}}\right)=\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{|\eta_{1}|^{l_{1}}|\eta_{2}|^{l_{2}}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{pn}|\eta_{2}|}\right).

(6.11)

This completes the analysis of
\mathsf{B}_{i}
in view of (
6.8
). For
\mathsf{L}_{i},
we summarize the results in the following lemma and put its proof into next subsection.

Lemma 6.3
.

For
\mathsf{L}_{i}
defined in (
6.3
), we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{1}{z_{2}(1+\sigma_{i}\phi^{-1/2}m(z_{2}))}\mathsf{L}_{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{1}{z_{2}(1+\sigma_{i}\phi^{-1/2}m(z_{2}))}\frac{-\kappa_{4}}{p}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\{l_{1}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}-1}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}-1}\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{1}}\left(\frac{-\sigma_{i}m(z_{1})}{1+\phi^{-1/2}m(z_{1})\sigma_{i}}\frac{-\sigma_{i}m(z_{2})}{1+\phi^{-1/2}m(z_{2})\sigma_{i}}\right)\right.

\displaystyle+\left.(l_{2}-1)((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}-2}\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{2}}\left(\frac{-\sigma_{i}m(z_{2})}{1+\phi^{-1/2}m(z_{2})\sigma_{i}}\right)^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{|\eta_{1}|^{l_{1}}|\eta_{2}|^{l_{2}}}\left(\frac{n^{1/4}}{p^{3/4}}\Psi(z_{2})+\frac{1}{p|\eta_{1}|}+\frac{1}{p|\eta_{2}|}+\phi^{-1/2}\frac{\sqrt{|\eta_{2}|}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\right).

Inserting Lemma
6.3
, (
6.8
), (
6.10
) and (
6.11
) into (
6.7
), we readily obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{1}{z_{2}(1+\sigma_{i}\phi^{-1/2}m(z_{2}))}\left\{\frac{\phi^{1/2}\sigma_{i}}{p(1+\phi^{-1/2}m(z_{2})\sigma_{i})}\mathbb{E}\left\{((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}}\right\}\right.

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{{\sqrt{pn}}}\mathbb{E}\left[l_{1}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}-1}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}-1}\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{1}}\left(\frac{z_{1}\sigma_{i}(\mathfrak{g}_{i}(z_{1})-\mathfrak{g_{i}}(z_{2}))}{z_{1}-z_{2}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{\sqrt{pn}}\mathbb{E}\left[(l_{2}-1)((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}-2}\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{2}}\left((\sigma_{i}\mathfrak{g}_{i}(z_{2})+\sigma_{i}z_{2}\mathfrak{g}_{i}^{\prime}(z_{2}))\right)\right]

\displaystyle-\frac{\kappa_{4}}{p}\mathbb{E}\left[l_{1}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}-1}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}-1}\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{1}}\left(\frac{-\sigma_{i}m(z_{1})}{1+\phi^{-1/2}m(z_{1})\sigma_{i}}\frac{-\sigma_{i}m(z_{2})}{1+\phi^{-1/2}m(z_{2})\sigma_{i}}\right)\right.

\displaystyle+\left.\left.(l_{2}-1)((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}-2}\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{2}}\left(\frac{-\sigma_{i}m(z_{2})}{1+\phi^{-1/2}m(z_{2})\sigma_{i}}\right)^{2}\right]\right\}

(6.12)

\displaystyle+\mathrm{O}\left(\frac{1}{|\eta_{1}|^{l_{1}}|\eta_{2}|^{l_{2}}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{np}|\eta_{1}|}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{np}|\eta_{2}|}\right)\right)+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{|\eta_{1}|^{l_{1}}|\eta_{2}|^{l_{2}}}\left(\frac{n^{1/4}}{p^{3/4}}\Psi(z_{2})+\frac{1}{p|\eta_{1}|}+\frac{1}{p|\eta_{2}|}+\phi^{-1/2}\frac{\sqrt{|\eta_{2}|}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\right).

We can further use Lemma
5.1
to simplify (
6.1
) and get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\phi^{1/2}\sigma_{i}}{z_{2}\left(1+\phi^{-1/2}{m}(z_{2})\sigma_{i}\right)^{2}}\right)^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{1}{z_{2}(1+\sigma_{i}\phi^{-1/2}m(z_{2}))}

\displaystyle\left\{-\frac{2}{{\sqrt{pn}}}\mathbb{E}\left[l_{1}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}-1}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}-1}\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{1}}\left(\frac{z_{1}\sigma_{i}(\mathfrak{g}_{i}(z_{1})-\mathfrak{g_{i}}(z_{2}))}{z_{1}-z_{2}}\right)\right]\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\frac{2}{\sqrt{np}}\mathbb{E}\left[(l_{2}-1)((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}-2}\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{2}}\left((\sigma_{i}\mathfrak{g}_{i}(z_{2})+\sigma_{i}z_{2}\mathfrak{g}_{i}^{\prime}(z_{2}))\right)\right]\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\frac{\kappa_{4}}{p}\mathbb{E}\left[l_{1}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}-1}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}-1}\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{1}}\left(\frac{-\sigma_{i}m(z_{1})}{1+\phi^{-1/2}m(z_{1})\sigma_{i}}\frac{-\sigma_{i}m(z_{2})}{1+\phi^{-1/2}m(z_{2})\sigma_{i}}\right)\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\left.(l_{2}-1)((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}-2}\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{2}}\left(\frac{-\sigma_{i}m(z_{2})}{1+\phi^{-1/2}m(z_{2})\sigma_{i}}\right)^{2}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle+\mathrm{O}\left(\frac{1}{|\eta_{1}|^{l_{1}}|\eta_{2}|^{l_{2}}}\left(\frac{1}{n|\eta_{1}|}+\frac{1}{n|\eta_{2}|}\right)\frac{1}{\sqrt{\kappa_{2}+\eta_{2}}}\right)+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{n^{3/4}p^{1/4}\min_{i=1}^{l}\{\sqrt{|\eta_{i}|(\kappa_{i}+|\eta_{i}|)}\}}\right)

\displaystyle+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{|\eta_{1}|^{l_{1}}|\eta_{2}|^{l_{2}}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right).

(6.13)

The above equation is already in the form of (
3.3
). Therefore, by Lemma
5.11
, the Gaussianity of the resolvents follows. The rest of this section is devoted to
more explicit formulas for the covariance as in Lemma
5.11
. Let
J_{1}
and
J_{3}
denote the first and the third terms on the right hand side of (
6.1
). In order to obtain
\omega(z_{1},z_{2}),
in view of (
3.3
), we only need to calculate
J_{1}
and
J_{3}
explicitly. Using Lemma
5.2
, we have that

\displaystyle z_{1}z_{2}\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\sigma_{i}\mathfrak{g}_{i}\left(z_{1}\right)\mathfrak{g}_{i}\left(z_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\sigma_{i}}{\left(1+\phi^{-1/2}{m}(z_{1})\sigma_{i}\right)\left(1+\phi^{-1/2}{m}(z_{2})\sigma_{i}\right)}=\frac{z_{1}{m}(z_{1})-z_{2}{m}(z_{2})}{\phi^{1/2}\left({m}(z_{1})-{m}(z_{2})\right)}

\displaystyle z_{1}^{2}\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\sigma_{i}\mathfrak{g}_{i}^{2}\left(z_{1}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\sigma_{i}}{\left(1+\phi^{-1/2}{m}_{1}\sigma_{i}\right)^{2}}=\frac{\left(z_{1}{m}_{1}\right)^{\prime}}{\phi^{1/2}{m}_{1}^{\prime}}.

Combining with the definitions of
J_{1}
and
J_{3},
we see that

\displaystyle J_{1}=

\displaystyle-\frac{z_{2}{m}^{\prime}(z_{2})}{{m}(z_{2})}\frac{2p}{{\sqrt{pn}}}\mathbb{E}\left[l_{1}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}-1}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}-1}\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{1}}\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\left(\mathfrak{g}_{i}(z_{2})\frac{z_{1}\sigma_{i}(\mathfrak{g}_{i}(z_{1})-\mathfrak{g_{i}}(z_{2}))}{z_{1}-z_{2}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle l_{1}\mathbb{E}\left[((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}-1}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}-1}\right]2\left(\frac{{m}^{\prime}(z_{1}){m}^{\prime}(z_{2})}{\left({m}(z_{1})-{m}(z_{2})\right)^{2}}-\frac{1}{\left(z_{1}-z_{2}\right)^{2}}\right),

\displaystyle J_{3}=

\displaystyle\frac{z_{2}{m}(z_{2})^{\prime}}{{m}(z_{2})}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{1}{z_{2}\left(1+\phi^{-1/2}{m}(z_{2})\sigma_{i}\right)}\frac{\kappa_{4}}{p}\mathbb{E}\left[l_{1}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}-1}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}-1}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{1}}\left(\frac{-\sigma_{i}m(z_{1})}{1+\phi^{-1/2}m(z_{1})\sigma_{i}}\frac{-\sigma_{i}m(z_{2})}{1+\phi^{-1/2}m(z_{2})\sigma_{i}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle l_{1}\frac{\kappa_{4}}{p}\mathbb{E}\left[((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}-1}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}-1}\right]\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{1}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{i}m(z_{1})}{1+\phi^{-1/2}m(z_{1})\sigma_{i}}\right)\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{i}m(z_{2})}{1+\phi^{-1/2}m(z_{2})\sigma_{i}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle l_{1}\mathbb{E}\left[((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}-1}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}-1}\right]

\displaystyle\times\kappa_{4}\phi\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial z_{1}\partial z_{2}}\left[\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{1}{\left(1+\phi^{-1/2}{m}(z_{1})\sigma_{i}\right)\left(1+\phi^{-1/2}{m}(z_{2})\sigma_{i}\right)}\right]\right).

This immediately yields that

\omega(z_{1},z_{2})=2\left(\frac{{m}^{\prime}(z_{1}){m}^{\prime}(z_{2})}{\left({m}(z_{1})-{m}(z_{2})\right)^{2}}-\frac{1}{\left(z_{1}-z_{2}\right)^{2}}\right)+\kappa_{4}\phi\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial z_{1}\partial z_{2}}\left(\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{1}{\left(1+\phi^{-1/2}{m}(z_{1})\sigma_{i}\right)\left(1+\phi^{-1/2}{m}(z_{2})\sigma_{i}\right)}\right).

This concludes the proof of (
3.3
) when
l=2.
For general
l,
the discussion is similar except for some notional modifications. In fact, we can conclude the prove using induction since we can expand
\mathcal{Y}\left(z_{1}\right)
as in (
6.2
) to get

\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{Y}\left(z_{1}\right)\cdots\mathcal{Y}\left(z_{l}\right)\right]=\sum_{s=2}^{l}\omega\left(z_{1},z_{s}\right)\mathbb{E}\prod_{t\notin\{1,s\}}\mathcal{Y}\left(z_{t}\right)+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(\mathsf{Re}\right).

(6.14)

We omit further details.

6.2
Proof of Lemma
6.3

Recall that
\mathsf{L}_{i}=\sum_{k=2}^{l_{0}}\mathsf{L}_{i}(k),
where
l_{0}=4
and

\mathsf{L}_{i}(k):=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sqrt{\sigma_{i}}\frac{\kappa_{k+1}}{(np)^{(k+1)/4}k!}\mathbb{E}\frac{\partial^{k}\left((1-\mathbb{E})[{((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}-1}}]({R_{1}}(z_{2})Y)_{ij}\right)}{\partial x_{ij}^{k}}.

First, when
k=2
, we have that

\mathsf{L}_{i}(2):=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sqrt{\sigma_{i}}\frac{\kappa_{3}}{(np)^{3/4}2}\mathbb{E}\frac{\partial^{2}\left((1-\mathbb{E})[{((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}-1}}]({R_{1}}(z_{2})Y)_{ij}\right)}{\partial x_{ij}^{2}}.

(6.15)

Due to similarity, we only focus on some representative terms. By chain rule, one of the terms in
\mathsf{L}_{i}(2)
is

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sqrt{\sigma_{i}}\frac{\kappa_{3}}{2(np)^{3/4}}\mathbb{E}\left((1-\mathbb{E})[{((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}-1}}]\frac{\partial^{2}({R_{1}}(z_{2})Y)_{ij}}{\partial x_{ij}^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sqrt{\sigma_{i}}\frac{\kappa_{3}}{2(np)^{3/4}}\mathbb{E}\left([{((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}-1}}](1-\mathbb{E})\frac{\partial^{2}({R_{1}}(z_{2})Y)_{ij}}{\partial x_{ij}^{2}}\right).

Using (
6.4
)–(
6.6
), we have that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}({R_{1}}Y)_{ij}}{\partial x_{ij}^{2}}=

\displaystyle\sigma_{i}\left\{-2({R_{1}})_{ii}({R_{1}}Y)_{ij}+2({R_{1}})_{ii}({R_{1}}Y)_{ij}(Y^{*}R_{1}Y)_{jj}\right.

(6.16)

\displaystyle-({R_{1}})_{ii}\left[2({R_{1}}Y)_{ij}-2({R_{1}}Y)_{ij}(Y^{*}{R_{1}}Y)_{jj}\right]

\displaystyle\left.-2\left[({R_{1}})_{ii}-({R_{1}})_{ii}(Y^{*}R_{1}Y)_{jj}-(R_{1}Y)_{ij}(R_{1}Y)_{ij}\right](R_{1}Y)_{ij}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma_{i}(-6({R_{1}})_{ii}({R_{1}}Y)_{ij}+6({R_{1}})_{ii}({R_{1}}Y)_{ij}(Y^{*}R_{1}Y)_{jj}+2(({R_{1}}Y)_{ij})^{3}).

Before proceeding to the actually control, we summarize some important observations. Note that every time when we take the derivatives with respect to
x_{ij},
it always generates some terms with one additional
i
and one additional
j
before further simplification. Consequently, we at least have one
({R_{1}}Y)_{ij}
factor, which can be well controlled Theorem
5.5
in the sense that
|(1-\mathbb{E})({R_{1}}Y)_{ij}|\prec\phi^{-1/4}\Psi(z)
.

We now control these terms. Recall (
6.9
). In view of (
6.7
), it suffices to control terms such as
\sum_{i=1}^{p}\mathfrak{g}_{i}(z_{2})(1-\mathbb{E})\sigma_{i}\sum_{j=1}^{n}[({R_{1}})_{ii}({R_{1}}Y)_{ij}(Y^{*}R_{1}Y)_{jj}](z_{2})
. Note that the diagonals can be replaced by their deterministic counterparts as in Theorem
5.5
. With a continuity argument similar to the proof of Theorem
5.5
(see Section 5.2 of
[
43
]
for more details), we can show that

(1-\mathbb{E})\sum_{i=1}^{p}\mathfrak{g}_{i}(z_{2})\sigma_{i}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left[\frac{({R_{1}})_{ii}}{\sqrt{p}}({R_{1}}Y)_{ij}\frac{(Y^{*}R_{1}Y)_{jj}}{\sqrt{n}}\right](z_{2})=\phi^{-1/2}\Psi(z_{2}).

Similarly we can handle the rest terms in (
6.16
). In summary, we can show that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{p}\mathfrak{g}_{i}(z_{2})\mathsf{L}_{i}(2)

(6.17)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{p}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sqrt{\sigma_{i}}\mathfrak{g}_{i}(z_{2})\frac{\kappa_{3}}{(np)^{3/4}2}\mathbb{E}\left([{((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}-1}}](1-\mathbb{E})\frac{\partial^{2}({R_{1}}(z_{2})Y)_{ij}}{\partial x_{ij}^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(\frac{(np)^{-3/4}}{|\eta_{1}|^{l_{1}}|\eta_{2}|^{l_{2}-1}}\phi^{-1/2}\sqrt{np}\frac{1}{|\eta_{2}|}\Psi(z_{2})\left(1+\frac{1}{(\sqrt{n|\eta_{2}|})^{2}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{|\eta_{1}|^{l_{1}}|\eta_{2}|^{l_{2}}}\frac{n^{1/4}}{p^{3/4}}\Psi(z_{2})\right).

Second, for
\mathsf{L}_{i}(3)
, by a discussion similar to (
6.17
), we have that

\displaystyle\mathsf{L}_{i}(3)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{\sqrt{\sigma_{i}}3\kappa_{4}}{3!np}\mathbb{E}\left\{\frac{\partial^{2}\left((1-\mathbb{E})[{((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}-1}}]\right)}{\partial x_{ij}^{2}}\frac{\partial{({{R_{1}}(z_{2})Y})_{ij}}}{\partial x_{ij}}\right\}

\displaystyle+\mathrm{O}\left(\frac{\Psi(z_{2})}{|\eta_{1}|^{l_{1}}|\eta_{2}|^{l_{2}-1}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{\sqrt{\sigma_{i}}\kappa_{4}}{2np}\mathbb{E}\left\{\frac{\partial^{2}[{((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}-1}}]}{\partial x_{ij}^{2}}\frac{\partial{({{R_{1}}(z_{2})Y})_{ij}}}{\partial x_{ij}}\right\}+\mathrm{O}\left(\frac{\Psi(z_{2})}{|\eta_{1}|^{l_{1}}|\eta_{2}|^{l_{2}-1}}\right).

For the first term on the right-hand side of the above equation, using (
6.4
) and (
6.5
), we find that it suffices to handle the following term

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{\sqrt{\sigma_{i}}\kappa_{4}}{2np}\mathbb{E}\left\{l_{1}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}-1}\frac{\partial[-2\sqrt{\sigma_{i}}\frac{\partial(R_{1}(z_{1})Y)_{ij}}{\partial z_{1}}]}{\partial X_{ij}}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}-1}\frac{\partial{({{R_{1}}(z_{2})Y})_{ij}}}{\partial x_{ij}}\right.

\displaystyle+\left.(l_{2}-1)((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}-2}\frac{\partial[-2\sqrt{\sigma_{i}}\frac{\partial(R_{2}(z_{2})Y)_{ij}}{\partial z_{2}}]}{\partial X_{ij}}\frac{\partial{({{R_{1}}(z_{2})Y})_{ij}}}{\partial x_{ij}}\right\}.

Using (
6.6
)
and
{R_{1}}Y=Y{R_{2}}
,
Y^{*}{R_{1}}={R_{2}}Y^{*}
, we can rewrite

({R_{1}})_{ii}-({R_{1}})_{ii}(Y^{*}R_{1}Y)_{jj}-(R_{1}Y)_{ij}(Y^{*}R_{1})_{ji}=-z({R_{2}})_{jj}({R_{1}})_{ii}-(R_{1}Y)_{ij}(Y^{*}R_{1})_{ji}.

Together with Theorem
5.5
, we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{\sqrt{\sigma_{i}}\kappa_{4}}{2np}\mathbb{E}\left\{l_{1}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}-1}\frac{\partial[-2\sqrt{\sigma_{i}}\frac{\partial(R_{1}(z_{1})Y)_{ij}}{\partial z_{1}}]}{\partial x_{ij}}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}-1}\frac{\partial{({{R_{1}}(z_{2})Y})_{ij}}}{\partial x_{ij}}\right.

\displaystyle+\left.(l_{2}-1)((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}-2}\frac{\partial[-2\sqrt{\sigma_{i}}\frac{\partial(R_{2}(z_{2})Y)_{ij}}{\partial z_{2}}]}{\partial x_{ij}}\frac{\partial{({{R_{1}}(z_{2})Y})_{ij}}}{\partial x_{ij}}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{-\kappa_{4}}{p}\left\{l_{1}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}-1}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}-1}\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{1}}\left(\frac{-\sigma_{i}m(z_{1})}{1+\phi^{-1/2}m(z_{1})\sigma_{i}}\frac{-\sigma_{i}m(z_{2})}{1+\phi^{-1/2}m(z_{2})\sigma_{i}}\right)\right.

\displaystyle+\left.(l_{2}-1)((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}-2}\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{2}}\left(\frac{-\sigma_{i}m(z_{2})}{1+\phi^{-1/2}m(z_{2})\sigma_{i}}\right)^{2}\right\}+\mathcal{E}_{L,3}(i),

where
\mathcal{E}_{L,3}(i)
is defined as

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{L,3}(i):

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{-\kappa_{4}}{p}\left\{l_{1}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}-1}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}-1}\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{1}}\right.

\displaystyle\left[\left(-z_{1}({R_{2}})_{jj}(z_{1})({R_{1}})_{ii}(z_{1})-(R_{1}Y)_{ij}(z_{1})(Y^{*}R_{1})_{ji}(z_{1})-\frac{-\sigma_{i}m(z_{1})}{1+\phi^{-1/2}m(z_{1})\sigma_{i}}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\left(-z_{2}({R_{2}})_{jj}(z_{2})({R_{1}})_{ii}(z_{2})-(R_{1}Y)_{ij}(z_{2})(Y^{*}R_{1})_{ji}(z_{2})-\frac{-\sigma_{i}m(z_{2})}{1+\phi^{-1/2}m(z_{2})\sigma_{i}}\right)\right]\right.

\displaystyle+(l_{2}-1)((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{1}))^{l_{1}}((1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z_{2}))^{l_{2}-2}\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{2}}\left(-z_{2}({R_{2}})_{jj}(z_{2})({R_{1}})_{ii}(z_{2})\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.-(R_{1}Y)_{ij}(z_{2})(Y^{*}R_{1})_{ji}(z_{2})-\frac{-\sigma_{i}m(z_{2})}{1+\phi^{-1/2}m(z_{2})\sigma_{i}}\right)^{2}\right\}.

In view of (
6.7
), we need to control
\sum_{i=1}^{p}\mathfrak{g}_{i}(z_{2})\mathcal{E}_{L,3}(i)
. This can be done via an argument similar to (
6.10
). We have that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{p}\mathfrak{g}_{i}(z_{2})\mathcal{E}_{L,3}(i)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{|\eta_{1}|^{l_{1}-1}|\eta_{2}|^{l_{2}-1}}\frac{1}{p}\frac{1}{|\eta_{1}|}\left(\frac{1}{|\eta_{1}|}+\Psi(z_{1})\right)\left(\frac{1}{|\eta_{2}|}+\Psi(z_{2})\right)+\frac{1}{|\eta_{1}|^{l_{1}}|\eta_{2}|^{l_{2}-2}}\frac{1}{p}\frac{1}{|\eta_{2}|}\left(\frac{1}{|\eta_{2}|}+\Psi(z_{2})\right)^{2}\right)

Finally, for
\mathsf{L}_{i}(4),
the discussions are analogous except that we need to handle higher order derivatives like
\partial^{3}(R_{1}Y)_{ij}/\partial x_{ij}^{3}.
We have that

\sum_{i=1}^{p}\mathfrak{g}_{i}(z_{2})\mathsf{L}_{i}(4)=\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{|\eta_{1}|^{l_{1}}|\eta_{2}|^{l_{2}}}\frac{\sqrt{\eta_{2}}}{\sqrt{p}}\right).

This completes our proof.

7
Proof of of the main theorems

For notional convenience, we denote that for
f_{i}(x)
in (
3.2
)

\mathcal{Z}_{{\eta_{0}},E}(f_{i}):=\operatorname{Tr}f_{i}\left(Q\right)-\mathbb{E}\operatorname{Tr}f_{i}\left(Q\right).

Recall (
3.17
). In what follows, we will study the deterministic mean bias part
\mathcal{M}_{\eta_{0},E}(f_{i})
and the random term
\mathcal{Z}_{\eta_{0},E}(f_{i}).
For simplicity, as before, we also denote that
Y:=\Sigma^{1/2}X
.

We point out that the analysis of
\mathcal{Z}_{\eta_{0},E}(f_{i})
is similar to that of Theorem
6.1
and the key is to prove the following lemma which is an analog of Lemma
6.2
. Recall (
3.6
).

Lemma 7.1
.

Suppose
X
and
\Sigma
satisfy Assumptions
2.1
. Then for
E\in\mathbf{R}
any
l\in\mathbb{N}
, we have that

\mathbb{E}\left[\prod_{j=1}^{l}\mathcal{Z}_{{\eta_{0}},E}\left(f_{i_{j}}\right)\right]=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\sum\prod\varpi\left(f_{i_{s}},f_{i_{t}}\right)+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(n^{-\mathsf{c}}\right),&\text{ if }l\in 2\mathbb{N}\\
\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(n^{-\mathsf{c}}\right),&\text{ otherwise }\end{array},\right.

for some constant
c>0
, where
{i_{j}}\in\{1,\ldots,\mathsf{K}\}
,
1\leq j\leq l
and
\sum\prod
means summing over all distinct ways of partitions into pairs. Here
\varpi\left(f_{i},f_{j}\right)\equiv\varpi_{n}\left(f_{i},f_{j}\right)
is defined as

\displaystyle\begin{cases}-\frac{1}{4\pi^{2}}\iint f_{i}\left(x_{1}\right)f_{j}\left(x_{2}\right)\alpha\left(x_{1},x_{2}\right)\mathrm{d}x_{1}\mathrm{d}x_{2}-\frac{1}{4\pi^{2}}\iint f_{i}\left(x_{1}\right)f_{j}\left(x_{2}\right)\beta\left(x_{1},x_{2}\right)\mathrm{d}x_{1}\mathrm{d}x_{2},&\text{ when $\eta_{0}\asymp 1$ }\\
-\frac{1}{4\pi^{2}}\iint f_{i}\left(x_{1}\right)f_{j}\left(x_{2}\right)\beta\left(x_{1},x_{2}\right)\mathrm{d}x_{1}\mathrm{d}x_{2},&\text{ when $\eta_{0}=\mathrm{o}(1)$ }\end{cases}.

(7.1)

7.1
Proof of Lemma
7.1

Throughout this section, to avoid confusion with the scaling parameters
\eta
’s, in this section we use
y
for
\operatorname{Im}z
and write
z=x+\mathrm{i}y.
According to the definition (
3.9
), we have that for
i=1,\ldots,\mathsf{K}

\|f_{i}\|_{1}=\mathrm{O}(\eta_{0}),\quad\left\|f_{i}^{\prime}\right\|_{1}=\mathrm{O}(1),\quad\left\|f_{i}^{\prime\prime}\right\|_{1}=\mathrm{O}(\eta_{0}^{-1}).

(7.2)

Proof.

Due to similarity, we mainly focus on a single test function and discuss how to generalize the results to
\mathsf{K}>1
in the end of the proof. In this case, it suffices to calculate
\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{Z}_{{\eta_{0}},E}^{l}(f)\right],l\in\mathbb{N}.
Similar to (
7.23
), the staring point is the following representation

\mathcal{Z}_{{\eta_{0}},E}(f)=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{C}}\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{z}}\tilde{f}(z)(\operatorname{Tr}({R_{1}}(z))-\mathbb{E}\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z))\mathrm{d}^{2}z.

(7.3)

In what follows, for notional simplicity, we write
\mathcal{Z}(f)\equiv\mathcal{Z}_{{\eta_{0}},E}(f).
We let

\tilde{{\eta}}:=n^{-\varepsilon_{0}}{\eta_{0}}\quad\underline{\eta}:=n^{-\varepsilon_{1}}{\eta_{0}}

(7.4)

for some small constants
\varepsilon_{1}>\varepsilon_{0}
, which we will choose later. To further apply Lemma
5.8
, we define the almost analytical extension of
f
more concretely as follows

\tilde{f}(z):=\left(f(x)+\mathrm{i}yf^{\prime}(x)\right)\chi\left(\frac{y}{\tilde{\eta}}\right).

We further denote that for
z=x+\mathrm{i}y

\theta_{f}(z):=\frac{1}{\pi}\frac{\partial\tilde{f}(z)}{\partial\bar{z}}=\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2\pi}yf^{\prime\prime}(x)\chi(y/\tilde{\eta})-\frac{1}{2\pi\tilde{\eta}}yf^{\prime}(x)\chi^{\prime}(y/\tilde{\eta})+\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2\pi\tilde{\eta}}f(x)\chi^{\prime}(y/\tilde{\eta}).

(7.5)

Together with (
7.3
) and the definition in (
6.1
), it suffices to calculate

\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{Z}(f)]^{l}=\int\theta_{f}\left(z_{1}\right)\cdots\theta_{f}\left(z_{l}\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{Y}\left(z_{1}\right)\cdots\mathcal{Y}\left(z_{l}\right)\right]\mathrm{d}^{2}z_{1}\cdots\mathrm{d}^{2}z_{l},

(7.6)

where
z_{i}:=x_{i}+\mathrm{i}y_{i},\ 1\leq i\leq l
. In what follows, we will calculate (
7.6
) using Lemma
6.2
by splitting the regions into ”good” one
\mathcal{R}
and ”bad” one
\mathcal{R}^{c}
where

\mathcal{R}:=\left\{z_{1},z_{2},\cdots,z_{l}\in\mathbb{C}:\left|y_{1}\right|,\cdots,\left|y_{l}\right|\in[\underline{\eta},2\tilde{{\eta}}]\right\}.

Control on the ”bad” region.
We now control the integral on the bad region
\mathcal{R}^{c}
assuming the following bound holds

|(1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z)|\prec\frac{1}{|\operatorname{Im}z|},

(7.7)

whose proof will be given in Section
B.3
after some necessary notations are introduced. We first bound on a single spectral parameter. In this case,
using the definition of
\mathcal{R}^{c},
we need to bound the following two integrals

\int_{|y|\leq\underline{\eta}}\left|\theta_{f}(z)\frac{1}{|y|}\right|\mathrm{d}^{2}z,\quad\int_{\underline{\eta}\leq|y|\leq 2\tilde{{\eta}}}\left|\theta_{f}(z)\frac{1}{|y|}\right|\mathrm{d}^{2}z.

For the first integral, note that by definition, we have
\theta_{f}(z)=0
for
|y|\geqslant 2\tilde{{\eta}}
and
\chi^{\prime}(y/\tilde{{\eta}})=0
for
|y|\leq\tilde{{\eta}}
. Together with (
7.2
), we get for some constant
C^{{}^{\prime\prime}}>0

\displaystyle\int_{|y|\leq\underline{\eta}}\left|\theta_{f}(z)\frac{1}{|y|}\right|\mathrm{d}^{2}z\leq\int_{|y|\leq\underline{\eta}}|f^{\prime\prime}(x)|\mathrm{d}^{2}z\leq{{\underline{\eta}}}\int_{|\tilde{y}|\leq 1}C^{\prime\prime}/{\eta_{0}}\mathrm{d}{x}\mathrm{d}\tilde{y}\leq n^{-\varepsilon_{1}}

(7.8)

where in the second step we used change of variable that
\tilde{y}:=y/\underline{\eta}.

For the second integral, by a discussion similar to (
7.8
), we can show that

\int_{\underline{\eta}\leq|y|\leq\tilde{{\eta}}}\left|\theta_{f}(z)\frac{1}{|y|}\right|\mathrm{d}^{2}z\leq C^{\prime\prime}n^{-\varepsilon_{0}}.

(7.9)

Moreover, we have that

\displaystyle\int_{\tilde{{\eta}}\leq|y|\leq 2\tilde{{\eta}}}\left|\theta_{f}(z)\frac{1}{|y|}\right|\mathrm{d}^{2}z

\displaystyle\leq n^{-\varepsilon_{0}}+\int_{\tilde{{\eta}}\leq|y|\leq 2\tilde{{\eta}}}\frac{1}{|y|\tilde{{\eta}}}\left|\left(f(x)+\mathrm{i}yf^{\prime}(x)\right)\chi^{\prime}(y/\tilde{{\eta}})\right|\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\leq Cn^{\varepsilon_{0}}.

(7.10)

This provides the controls for a single spectral parameter. For the integral (
7.6
), using the above estimates, it is not hard to see that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{R}^{c}}\theta_{f}\left(z_{1}\right)\cdots\theta_{f}\left(z_{l}\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{Y}\left(z_{1}\right)\cdots\mathcal{Y}\left(z_{l}\right)\right]\mathrm{d}^{2}z_{1}\cdots\mathrm{d}^{2}z_{l}

(7.11)

\displaystyle\prec

\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{l}\int_{\left|y_{s}\right|\leqslant\underline{\eta}}\prod_{i=1}^{l}\left|\phi_{f}\left(z_{i}\right)\frac{1}{|y_{i}|}\right|\mathrm{d}^{2}z_{1}\cdots\mathrm{d}^{2}z_{l}\lesssim n^{-\varepsilon_{1}}\cdot n^{(l-1)\varepsilon_{0}}\leqslant n^{-\varepsilon_{1}/2},

by choosing
(2l+2)\varepsilon_{0}<\varepsilon_{1}<\tau_{1}/3
. This shows that the integral in (
7.6
) over the ”bad” region
\mathcal{R}^{c}
is negligible.

Calculations on the ”good” region.
Combining (
7.6
) with (
7.11
), we see that

\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{Z}(f)]^{l}=\int_{\mathcal{R}}\theta_{f}\left(z_{1}\right)\cdots\theta_{f}\left(z_{l}\right)\mathbb{E}\mathfrak{G}\mathrm{d}^{2}z_{1}\cdots\mathrm{d}^{2}z_{l}+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(n^{-\varepsilon_{1}/2}\right),

(7.12)

where we abbreviate
\mathfrak{G}:=\mathcal{Y}\left(z_{1}\right)\cdots\mathcal{Y}\left(z_{l}\right)
. By
Lemma
6.2
and its proof (c.f. (
6.14
)), we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\mathfrak{G}=\omega\left(z_{1},z_{s}\right)\mathbb{E}\prod_{t\notin\{1,s\}}\mathcal{Y}\left(z_{t}\right)

(7.13)

\displaystyle+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{\prod_{i=1}^{l}|y_{i}|}\left(\frac{1}{n\min_{i=1}^{l}\{|y_{i}|\sqrt{\kappa_{i}+|y_{i}|}\}}+\frac{1}{n^{3/4}p^{1/4}\min_{i=1}^{l}\{\sqrt{|\eta_{i}|(\kappa_{i}+|\eta_{i}|)}\}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\right)

Plugging (
7.13
) in (
7.12
) and using (
7.2
), (
7.9
) and (
7.10
) to bound the error terms, we obtain that for
l\geqslant 2
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{Z}(f)]^{l}

\displaystyle=(l-1)\left(\int_{\underline{\eta}\leq\left|y_{1}\right|,\left|y_{s}\right|\leq 2\tilde{{\eta}}}\theta_{f}\left(z_{1}\right)\theta_{f}\left(z_{s}\right)\omega\left(z_{1},z_{s}\right)\mathrm{d}^{2}z_{1}\mathrm{~d}^{2}z_{s}\right)\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{Z}(f)]^{l-2}

(7.14)

\displaystyle+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(n^{-\varepsilon_{1}/2}+n^{l\varepsilon_{0}}(n\underline{\eta}\min_{i=1}^{l}\sqrt{\kappa_{i}+\underline{\eta}})^{-1}\right),

where
\underline{\eta}=n^{-\varepsilon_{1}}{\eta_{0}}\geqslant n^{-1+\tau_{1}-\varepsilon_{1}}
. Since we have chosen the constants such that
(2l+2)\varepsilon_{0}<\varepsilon_{1}<\tau_{1}/3
, we can bound
n^{l\varepsilon_{0}}(n\underline{\eta}\min_{i=1}^{l}\sqrt{\kappa_{i}+\underline{\eta}})^{-1}\leq n^{l\varepsilon_{0}+3\varepsilon_{1}/2}(n\eta_{0}\min_{i=1}^{l}\sqrt{\kappa_{i}+\eta_{0}})^{-1}\leq n^{2\varepsilon_{1}}n^{-\tau_{1}}\leq n^{-\varepsilon_{1}}
.
It remains to study the factor

\operatorname{Var}(\mathcal{Z}):=\int_{\underline{\eta}\leq\left|y_{1}\right|,\left|y_{2}\right|\leq 2\tilde{{\eta}}}\theta_{f}\left(z_{1}\right)\theta_{f}\left(z_{2}\right)\omega\left(z_{1},z_{2}\right)\mathrm{d}^{2}z_{1}\mathrm{~d}^{2}z_{2},

(7.15)

where we have taken
s=2
and abbreviated

\omega\left(z_{1},z_{2}\right)=\widehat{\alpha}\left(z_{1},z_{2}\right)+\widehat{\beta}\left(z_{1},z_{2}\right).

Next, we translate the regional integral (
7.15
) into a contour integral which can be naturally done with Green’s theorem. In order to apply Lemma
5.9
, note that by the fact that
m(z)
is holomprphic in
\mathbb{C}\backslash[\gamma_{-},\gamma_{+}]
, fix
z_{1}
, we can see that
\omega(z_{1},z_{2})
is holomorphic with respect to
z_{2}
except on
[\gamma_{-},\gamma_{+}]
and a neighborhood
\mathsf{B}_{\epsilon}(z_{1})
of
z_{1}
for a small enough
\epsilon=\epsilon(n)
such that
\mathsf{B}_{\epsilon}(z_{1})
will not cross any existing boundaries. This readily implies that

\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{z}_{2}}\omega(z_{1},z_{2})=0.

Similarly, we have that

\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{z}_{1}}\omega(z_{1},z_{2})=0,\quad\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{z}_{1}}\int\omega(z_{1},z_{2})\mathrm{d}z_{2}=0.

On the one hand, applying Lemma
5.9
to (
7.15
) twice, we have that

\displaystyle\operatorname{Var}(\mathcal{Z})=-\frac{1}{4\pi^{2}}\oint\oint\tilde{f}(z_{1})\tilde{f}(z_{2})\omega(z_{1},z_{2})\mathrm{d}z_{1}\mathrm{d}z_{2},

where the contour for
z_{2}
is
\partial\Omega_{2}^{0}\cup\partial\mathsf{B}_{\epsilon}(z_{1})
with

\Omega_{2}^{0}:=\{z_{2}=x_{2}+\mathrm{i}y_{2}:\underline{\eta}\leqslant|y_{2}|\leqslant 2\tilde{\eta}\}.

(7.16)

On the other hand, we can rewrite
\omega(z_{1},z_{2})
as

\omega(z_{1},z_{2})=\frac{2}{(z_{2}-z_{1})^{2}}\left(\frac{m^{\prime}(z_{1})m^{\prime}(z_{2})}{m(z_{1})m(z_{2})}-\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\sigma_{i}^{2}m^{\prime}(z_{1})m^{\prime}(z_{2})}{(1+\phi^{-1/2}\sigma_{i}m(z_{1}))(1+\phi^{-1/2}\sigma_{i}m(z_{2}))}-1\right).

Note that there are no singularities of the first order in
\mathsf{B}_{\epsilon}(z_{1})
. Moreover, by definition,
\tilde{f}(z)\equiv 0
when
\operatorname{Im}z=2\tilde{\eta}
. Therefore, we can simply take the contour for
z_{2}
as
\{z_{2}=x_{2}+\mathrm{i}y_{2}:|y_{2}|=\underline{\eta}\}
. Similarly, we can take the contour of
z_{1}
as
\{z_{1}=x_{1}+\mathrm{i}y_{1}:|y_{1}|=\underline{\eta}\}
. For simplicity, we choose the two contours which do not overlap. Without loss of generality, we denote
\underline{\eta}_{2}:=\underline{\eta}_{1}/2:=\underline{\eta}/2
and

\Gamma_{s}:=\{z_{s}=x_{s}+\mathrm{i}y_{s}:|y_{s}|=\underline{\eta}_{s}\},\quad s=1,2.

Based on the above discussion, it is easy to see that we can rewrite

\operatorname{Var}(\mathcal{Z})=-\frac{1}{4\pi^{2}}\oint_{\Gamma_{2}}\oint_{\Gamma_{1}}\tilde{f}(z_{1})\tilde{f}(z_{2})\omega(z_{1},z_{2})\mathrm{d}z_{1}\mathrm{d}z_{2}.

(7.17)

To conclude the proof, it suffices to show the equivalence of (
7.17
) with (
7.1
). The starting point is to rewrite (
7.17
) as follows

\operatorname{Var}(\mathcal{Z})=\mathcal{K}_{++}+\mathcal{K}_{--}-\mathcal{K}_{+-}-\mathcal{K}_{-+},

(7.18)

where the term
\mathcal{K}_{++}
contains the integral over the region
\mathcal{R}_{++}:=\left\{y_{1}=\underline{{\eta}}_{1},y_{2}=\underline{{\eta}}_{2}\right\}
,
\mathcal{K}_{--}
contains that of
\mathcal{R}_{--}:=\left\{y_{1}=-\underline{{\eta}}_{1},y_{2}=-\underline{{\eta}}_{2}\right\}
and
\mathcal{K}_{+-},\mathcal{K}_{-+}
are defined similarly. Note that

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{++}=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4\pi^{2}}\int\int\tilde{f}\left(z_{1}\right)\tilde{f}\left(z_{2}\right)\left(\widehat{\alpha}\left(x_{1}+\mathrm{i}\underline{\eta}_{1},x_{2}+\mathrm{i}\underline{\eta}_{2}\right)+\widehat{\beta}\left(x_{1}+\mathrm{i}\underline{\eta}_{1},x_{2}+\mathrm{i}\underline{\eta}_{2}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}z_{1}\mathrm{d}z_{2}

(7.19)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4\pi^{2}}\int\int\tilde{f}\left(x_{1}\right)\tilde{f}\left(x_{2}\right)\left(\widehat{\alpha}\left(x_{1}+\mathrm{i}\underline{\eta}_{1},x_{2}+\mathrm{i}\underline{\eta}_{2}\right)+\widehat{\beta}\left(x_{1}+\mathrm{i}\underline{\eta}_{1},x_{2}+\mathrm{i}\underline{\eta}_{2}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}x_{1}\mathrm{d}x_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4\pi^{2}}\int\int\left({f}\left(x_{1}\right)+\mathrm{i}\underline{\eta}_{1}f^{\prime}(x_{1})\right)\left({f}\left(x_{2}\right)+\mathrm{i}\underline{\eta}_{2}f^{\prime}(x_{2})\right)\left(\widehat{\alpha}\left(x_{1}+\mathrm{i}\underline{\eta}_{1},x_{2}+\mathrm{i}\underline{\eta}_{2}\right)+\widehat{\beta}\left(x_{1}+\mathrm{i}\underline{\eta}_{1},x_{2}+\mathrm{i}\underline{\eta}_{2}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}x_{1}\mathrm{d}x_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4\pi^{2}}\int\int{f}\left(x_{1}\right){f}\left(x_{2}\right)\left(\widehat{\alpha}\left(x_{1}+\mathrm{i}\underline{\eta}_{1},x_{2}+\mathrm{i}\underline{\eta}_{2}\right)+\widehat{\beta}\left(x_{1}+\mathrm{i}\underline{\eta}_{1},x_{2}+\mathrm{i}\underline{\eta}_{2}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}x_{1}\mathrm{d}x_{2}+\mathrm{O}(n^{-\varepsilon_{1}}\eta_{0}).

The other terms can be studied similarly. The main differences lie in the signs of the imaginary parts. Note that
\underline{\eta_{1}}\asymp\underline{\eta}_{2}\asymp\underline{\eta}=n^{-\varepsilon_{1}}\eta_{0}
and
\mathrm{d}z_{1}=\mathrm{d}x_{1},

\mathrm{d}z_{2}=\mathrm{d}x_{2}
. Then we find that when the imaginary part goes to zero and the limit exists, we have

\operatorname{Var}(\mathcal{Z})=-\frac{1}{4\pi^{2}}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}f\left(x_{1}\right)f\left(x_{2}\right)\alpha\left(x_{1},x_{2}\right)\mathrm{d}x_{1}\mathrm{d}x_{2}-\frac{1}{4\pi^{2}}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}f\left(x_{1}\right)f\left(x_{2}\right)\beta\left(x_{1},x_{2}\right)\mathrm{d}x_{1}\mathrm{d}x_{2}.

This concludes the proof for
\eta_{0}\asymp 1.

For
\eta_{0}=\mathrm{o}(1),
we apply the following change of variable

\tilde{x}_{i}=(x_{i}-E)/\eta_{0},\ i=1,2,

(7.20)

to the last step of (
7.19
) and get

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4\pi^{2}}\int\int{f}\left(x_{1}\right){f}\left(x_{2}\right)\left(\widehat{\alpha}\left(x_{1}+\mathrm{i}\underline{\eta}_{1},x_{2}+\mathrm{i}\underline{\eta}_{2}\right)+\widehat{\beta}\left(x_{1}+\mathrm{i}\underline{\eta}_{1},x_{2}+\mathrm{i}\underline{\eta}_{2}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}x_{1}\mathrm{d}x_{2}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\eta_{0}^{2}}{4\pi^{2}}\int\int{g}\left(\tilde{x}_{1}\right){g}\left(\tilde{x}_{2}\right)\left(\widehat{\alpha}\left(E+\tilde{x}_{1}{\eta_{0}}+\mathrm{i}\underline{\eta}_{1},E+\tilde{x}_{2}{\eta_{0}}+\mathrm{i}\underline{\eta}_{2}\right)+\widehat{\beta}\left(E+\tilde{x}_{1}{\eta_{0}}+\mathrm{i}\underline{\eta}_{1},E+\tilde{x}_{2}{\eta_{0}}+\mathrm{i}\underline{\eta}_{2}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}_{1}\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}_{2}

\displaystyle:=\left(\mathcal{K}_{1}\right)_{++}+\left(\mathcal{K}_{2}\right)_{++}.

For
(\mathcal{K}_{1})_{++},
we see that

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{K}_{1}\right)_{++}

\displaystyle=-\frac{{\eta_{0}}^{2}}{4\pi^{2}}\iint g\left(\tilde{x}_{1}\right)g\left(\tilde{x}_{2}\right)\widehat{\alpha}_{++}\left(E+\tilde{x}_{1}{\eta_{0}},E+\tilde{x}_{2}{\eta_{0}}\right)\mathrm{d}\tilde{y}_{1}\mathrm{~d}\tilde{y}_{2}+\mathrm{O}(\sqrt{\tilde{{\eta}}})

\displaystyle=-\frac{{\eta_{0}}^{2}}{4\pi^{2}}\iint g\left(\tilde{x}_{1}\right)g\left(\tilde{x}_{2}\right)\widehat{\alpha}_{++}\left(E+\tilde{x}_{1}{\eta_{0}},E+\tilde{x}_{2}{\eta_{0}}\right)\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}_{1}\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}_{2}+\mathrm{O}\left(n^{-\varepsilon_{0}/2}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{O}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\kappa_{1}+\underline{\eta}_{1}}\sqrt{\kappa_{2}+\underline{\eta}_{2}}}\eta_{0}^{2}\right)=\mathrm{O}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\underline{\eta}_{1}}\sqrt{\underline{\eta}_{2}}}\eta_{0}^{2}\right).

(7.21)

This indicates that when
\eta_{0}=\mathrm{o}(1)
, only the
\beta
terms will have non-vanishing contributions. Similarly, we can calculate the integral in
\mathcal{K}_{1}
over the other three regions:
\left(\mathcal{K}_{1}\right)_{+-}
for
\mathcal{R}_{+-}:=\left\{y_{1}=\underline{{\eta}}_{1},y_{2}=-\underline{{\eta}}_{2}\right\}
,
\left(\mathcal{K}_{1}\right)_{-+}
for
\mathcal{R}_{-+}:=\left\{y_{1}=-\underline{{\eta}}_{1},y_{2}=\underline{{\eta}}_{2}\right\}
, and
\left(\mathcal{K}_{1}\right)_{--}
for
\mathcal{R}_{--}:=\left\{y_{1}=-\underline{{\eta}}_{1},y_{2}=-\underline{{\eta}}_{2}\right\}
and obtain the same result. This completes the proof for
\eta_{0}=\mathrm{o}(1)
and hence that of
\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{Z}(f)^{l}].

Finally, we discuss how to generalize the results to multiple functions. Similar to (
7.3
), we can write

\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{Z}_{{\eta_{0}},E}\left(f_{i_{1}}\right)\cdots\mathcal{Z}_{{\eta_{0}},E}\left(f_{i_{l}}\right)\right]=\int\theta_{f_{i_{1}}}\left(z_{1}\right)\cdots\theta_{f_{i_{l}}}\left(z_{l}\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{Y}\left(z_{1}\right)\cdots\mathcal{Y}\left(z_{l}\right)\right]\mathrm{d}^{2}z_{1}\cdots\mathrm{d}^{2}z_{l}.

Following the proof for a single function, it is not hard to see that

\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{Z}_{{\eta_{0}},E}\left(f_{i_{1}}\right)\cdots\mathcal{Z}_{{\eta_{0}},E}\left(f_{i_{l}}\right)\right]=\sum_{s=2}^{l}\varpi\left(f_{i_{1}},f_{i_{s}}\right)\mathbb{E}\prod_{t\notin\{1,s\}}\mathcal{Z}_{{\eta_{0}},E}\left(f_{i_{t}}\right)+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(n^{-c}\right)

for some constant
c>0
. Then the proof follows from an induction argument.

∎

7.2
Proof of Theorems
3.2
and
3.7

Control of
\mathcal{M}_{\eta_{0},E}(f_{i})
.
For
simplicity, we first rewrite the quantity a little bit. Using the fact that
Q
and
\mathcal{Q}
share the same non-zero eigenvalues and
E\sim\sqrt{\phi}
and
g_{i}\in\mathcal{C}^{2}_{c}\left(\mathbb{R}\right),
we have that
f_{i}(0)=g_{i}(\frac{0-E}{\eta_{0}})
=0. Combining (
5.5
), we have that

n\int_{\mathbb{R}}f_{i}(x)\mathrm{d}\varrho(x)=p\int_{\mathbb{R}}f_{i}(x)\mathrm{d}\varrho_{p}(x).
Together with the definition of Stieltjes transform and Fubini’s theorem, using Lemma
5.8
, we have that

n\int_{\mathbb{R}}f_{i}(x)\mathrm{d}\varrho(x)=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{C}}\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{z}}\tilde{f}_{i}(z)pm_{p}(z)\mathrm{d}^{2}z.

(7.22)

Using Lemma
5.8
again, we have that

\mathbb{E}\operatorname{Tr}f_{i}(Q)=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{C}}\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{z}}\tilde{f}_{i}(z)\mathbb{E}\operatorname{Tr}R_{1}(z)\mathrm{d}^{2}z.

(7.23)

Therefore, due to the regularity properties of
f_{i},
in order to study the quantity
\mathcal{M}_{\eta_{0},E}
, it suffices to study
\mathbb{E}\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z)-pm_{p}(z)
and then apply a complex integral. The control of
\mathbb{E}\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z)-pm_{p}(z)
is similar to the discussions in the proof of Lemma
6.2
using the cumulant expansion formula Lemma
5.7
. Due to similarity, we only sketch the main ideas of the proof. We start with
\mathbb{E}({R_{1}})_{ii},1\leq i\leq p
. Using

\frac{\partial({R_{1}})_{ab}}{\partial y_{ij}}=-({R_{1}})_{ai}\left(Y^{*}{R_{1}}\right)_{jb}-({R_{1}}Y)_{aj}({R_{1}})_{ib},\quad\frac{\partial({R_{1}})_{ab}}{\partial x_{ij}}=\frac{\partial({R_{1}})_{ab}}{\partial y_{ij}}\sqrt{\sigma_{i}},

the relation
z{R_{1}}(z)=QR_{1}(z)-I
, Lemma
5.7
and Theorem
5.5
, we have that

\displaystyle z\mathbb{E}({R_{1}})_{ii}=\sqrt{\sigma_{i}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}X_{ij}({R_{1}}Y)_{ij}-1

(7.24)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{\sigma_{i}}}{\sqrt{np}}\mathbb{E}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{\partial({R_{1}}Y)_{ij}}{\partial x_{ij}}-1+\frac{\sqrt{\sigma_{i}}}{2!(np)^{\frac{3}{4}}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\kappa_{3}\mathbb{E}\frac{\partial^{2}({R_{1}}Y)_{ij}}{\partial^{2}x_{ij}}+\frac{\sqrt{\sigma_{i}}}{3!np}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\kappa_{4}\mathbb{E}\frac{\partial^{3}({R_{1}}Y)_{ij}}{\partial^{3}x_{ij}}+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{4}}p^{-\frac{5}{4}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\sigma_{i}}{\sqrt{np}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}({R_{1}})_{ii}-\frac{\sigma_{i}}{\sqrt{np}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left(Y^{*}({R_{1}})Y\right)_{jj}({R_{1}})_{ii}-\frac{\sigma_{i}}{\sqrt{np}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left((({R_{1}})Y)_{ij}\right)^{2}-1

\displaystyle+\frac{\sigma_{i}^{3/2}}{2(np)^{\frac{3}{4}}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\kappa_{3}\mathbb{E}\left(-6({R_{1}})_{ii}(({R_{1}})Y)_{ij}+6({R_{1}})_{ii}(({R_{1}})Y)_{ij}\left(Y^{*}({R_{1}})Y\right)_{jj}+2\left((({R_{1}})Y)_{ij}\right)^{3}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{\sigma_{i}^{2}}{6np}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\kappa_{4}\mathbb{E}\left(-6\left(({R_{1}})_{ii}\right)^{2}+12\left(({R_{1}})_{ii}\right)^{2}\left(Y^{*}({R_{1}})Y\right)_{jj}-6\left(({R_{1}})_{ii}\right)^{2}\left(\left(Y^{*}({R_{1}})Y\right)_{jj}\right)^{2}\right)+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(p^{-1}\Psi(z)\right)

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle=\mathsf{S}_{i,1}+\mathsf{S}_{i,2}+\mathsf{S}_{i,3}+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(p^{-1}\Psi(z)\right).

Then we deal with the terms
\mathsf{S}_{i,k},k=1,2,3.
First, by a discussion similar to the proof of Lemma
6.3
, using Theorem
5.5
, we have that

\displaystyle\mathsf{S}_{i,1}=

\displaystyle\phi^{-1/2}\sigma_{i}\mathbb{E}({R_{1}})_{ii}-\frac{\sigma_{i}}{\sqrt{np}}\mathbb{E}\left[\operatorname{Tr}\left(Y^{*}{R_{1}}Y\right)({R_{1}})_{ii}\right]-\frac{\sigma_{i}}{\sqrt{np}}\mathbb{E}(R_{1}QR_{1})_{ii}-1

(7.25)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\phi^{-1/2}\sigma_{i}\mathbb{E}({R_{1}})_{ii}-\frac{\sigma_{i}}{\sqrt{np}}\mathbb{E}\left[\operatorname{Tr}\left((Q-z+z){R_{1}}\right)({R_{1}})_{ii}\right]-\frac{\sigma_{i}}{\sqrt{np}}\mathbb{E}(R_{1}(Q-z+z)R_{1})_{ii}-1

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma_{i}(\phi^{-1/2}-\phi^{1/2})\mathbb{E}({R_{1}})_{ii}-z\sigma_{i}\phi^{1/2}\mathbb{E}\left[p^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}({R_{1}})({R_{1}})_{ii}\right]-\frac{\sigma_{i}}{\sqrt{np}}\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial z}z{R_{1}}(z)\right)_{ii}-1

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma_{i}(\phi^{-1/2}-\phi^{1/2})\mathbb{E}({R_{1}})_{ii}-z\sigma_{i}\phi^{1/2}m_{p}\mathbb{E}\left(({R_{1}})_{ii}+\frac{1}{z\left(1+\phi^{-1/2}m\sigma_{i}\right)}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{\sigma_{i}\phi^{1/2}}{p\left(1+\phi^{-1/2}m\sigma_{i}\right)}\mathbb{E}\operatorname{Tr}({R_{1}})-\frac{\sigma_{i}^{2}}{p}\frac{m^{\prime}}{\left(1+\phi^{-1/2}\sigma_{i}m\right)^{2}}-1+\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{i,1},

where we denote

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{i,1}:=

\displaystyle\frac{z\sigma_{i}\phi^{1/2}}{p}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\operatorname{Tr}({R_{1}(z)})-pm_{p}\right)\left(({R_{1}})_{ii}-\left(-\frac{1}{z(1+\phi^{-1/2}m\sigma_{i})}\right)\right)\right]

\displaystyle-\frac{\sigma_{i}}{\sqrt{np}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial z}z{R_{1}}(z)\right)_{ii}-\frac{\phi^{-1/2}\sigma_{i}m^{\prime}}{(1+\phi^{-1/2}m\sigma_{i})^{2}}\right).

Here we used the identity
\frac{\partial(zR(z))}{\partial z}={R_{1}}(z)+z{R_{1}}^{2}(z)
to estimate
\frac{\sigma_{i}}{\sqrt{np}}\mathbb{E}[({R_{1}}Q{R_{1}})_{ii}]
in the second step, and used the identity
\mathbb{E}[AB]=\mathbb{E}[(A-a)(B-b)]+\mathbb{E}[aB-ab+Ab]

to control
-z\sigma_{i}\phi^{1/2}\mathbb{E}\left[p^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}({R_{1}})({R_{1}})_{ii}\right]
in the last step. Plugging (
7.25
) into (
7.24
), we have

\displaystyle z\mathbb{E}({R_{1}})_{ii}=

\displaystyle\sigma_{i}(\phi^{-1/2}-\phi^{1/2})\mathbb{E}({R_{1}})_{ii}-z\sigma_{i}\phi^{1/2}m_{p}\mathbb{E}\left(({R_{1}})_{ii}+\frac{1}{z\left(1+\phi^{-1/2}m\sigma_{i}\right)}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{\sigma_{i}\phi^{1/2}}{p\left(1+\phi^{-1/2}m\sigma_{i}\right)}\mathbb{E}\operatorname{Tr}({R_{1}})-\frac{\sigma_{i}^{2}}{p}\frac{m^{\prime}}{\left(1+\phi^{-1/2}\sigma_{i}m\right)^{2}}-1+\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{i,1}+\mathsf{S}_{i,2}+\mathsf{S}_{i,3}+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(p^{-1}\Psi(z)\right).

Using (
5.5
), we can rewrite the above equation as follows

\displaystyle z(1+\sigma_{i}\phi^{-1/2}m)\mathbb{E}({R_{1}})_{ii}

(7.26)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\sigma_{i}\phi^{1/2}m_{p}}{\left(1+\phi^{-1/2}m\sigma_{i}\right)}+\frac{\sigma_{i}\phi^{1/2}}{p\left(1+\phi^{-1/2}m\sigma_{i}\right)}\mathbb{E}\operatorname{Tr}({R_{1}})-\frac{\sigma_{i}^{2}}{p}\frac{m^{\prime}}{\left(1+\phi^{-1/2}\sigma_{i}m\right)^{2}}-1

\displaystyle+\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{i,1}+\mathsf{S}_{i,2}+\mathsf{S}_{i,3}+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(p^{-1}\Psi(z)\right).

This yields that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\operatorname{Tr}({R_{1}})

(7.27)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{1}{z(1+\sigma_{i}\phi^{-1/2}m)}\left(\frac{\sigma_{i}\phi^{1/2}}{p\left(1+\phi^{-1/2}m\sigma_{i}\right)}\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Tr}({R_{1}})-pm_{p}]-\frac{\sigma_{i}^{2}}{p}\frac{m^{\prime}}{\left(1+\phi^{-1/2}\sigma_{i}m\right)^{2}}-1\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{i,1}+\mathsf{S}_{i,2}+\mathsf{S}_{i,3}+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(p^{-1}\Psi(z)\right)\right).

For the error term
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{i,1}
, using
\frac{1}{z(1+\sigma_{i}\phi^{-1/2}m)}\asymp\phi^{-1/2}
and a fluctuation averaging argument as in the proof of (
5.17
) (c.f. Lemma
A.9
), we can obtain that

\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{1}{z(1+\sigma_{i}\phi^{-1/2}m)}\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{i,1}=\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}p^{-\frac{1}{2}}\eta^{-2}\right).

(7.28)

For the summation involving
\mathsf{S}_{i,2},
among all the terms, we discuss the following representative term

(np)^{-3/4}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\sum_{j=1}^{n}({R_{1}})_{ii}(({R_{1}})Y)_{ij}\left(Y^{*}({R_{1}})Y\right)_{jj}\prec\phi^{1/2}(np)^{-3/4}(np)^{1/2}\phi^{-1/2}\Psi(z)=(np)^{-1/4}\Psi(z),

(7.29)

where we used the identities
{R_{1}}Y=Y{R_{2}}
,
Y^{*}{R_{1}}={R_{2}}Y^{*}
and (
5.18
). The other terms can be handled analogously (and more easily). This results in

\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{1}{z(1+\sigma_{i}\phi^{-1/2}m)}\mathsf{S}_{i,2}=\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(n^{\frac{1}{4}}p^{-\frac{3}{4}}\Psi(z)\right).

(7.30)

Finally, using Theorem
5.5
and similar arguments as in (
7.29
), we see that

\displaystyle\mathsf{S}_{i,3}=-\kappa_{4}\frac{\sigma_{i}^{2}}{p}\left(\frac{m^{2}}{\left(1+\phi^{-1/2}m\sigma_{i}\right)^{2}}\right)+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(p^{-1}\Psi(z)\right).

(7.31)

Inserting (
7.28
) (
7.30
) (
7.31
) back into (
7.27
) and use
z\left(1+\phi^{-1/2}m\sigma_{i}\right)\asymp\sqrt{\phi}
again, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\operatorname{Tr}({R_{1}})-\sum_{i=1}^{p}\left(-\frac{1}{z\left(1+\phi^{-1/2}m\sigma_{i}\right)}\right)

(7.32)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{z\left(1+\phi^{-1/2}m\sigma_{i}\right)}\left[\frac{\sigma_{i}\phi^{1/2}}{1+\phi^{-1/2}m\sigma_{i}}\mathbb{E}\left[p^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}({R_{1}})-m_{p}\right]-{\frac{1}{p}\frac{\sigma_{i}^{2}m^{\prime}}{\left(1+\phi^{-1/2}\sigma_{i}m\right)^{2}}}-\kappa_{4}\frac{1}{p}\frac{\sigma_{i}^{2}m^{2}}{\left(1+\phi^{-1/2}m\sigma_{i}\right)^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}p^{-\frac{1}{2}}\eta^{-2}+n^{\frac{1}{4}}p^{-\frac{3}{4}}\Psi(z)+\phi^{-1/2}\Psi(z)\right),

Using Lemma
5.2
, we can rewrite the above equation as follows

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\phi^{1/2}\sigma_{i}}{z\left(1+\phi^{-1/2}m(z)\sigma_{i}\right)^{2}}\right)\mathbb{E}\left(\operatorname{Tr}({R_{1}})-pm_{p}\right)

(7.33)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle-\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\sigma_{i}^{2}m^{\prime}}{z\left(1+\phi^{-1/2}m\sigma_{i}\right)^{3}}}-\kappa_{4}\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\sigma_{i}^{2}m^{2}}{z\left(1+\phi^{-1/2}m\sigma_{i}\right)^{3}}+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}p^{-\frac{1}{2}}\eta^{-2}+n^{\frac{1}{4}}p^{-\frac{3}{4}}\Psi(z)+\phi^{-1/2}\Psi(z)\right).

Combining (
5.7
) and (
5.9
) with a straightforward calculation, we obtain that

\mathbb{E}(\operatorname{Tr}({R_{1}})-pm_{p})=b(z)+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(\frac{n^{-1}\eta^{-2}+n^{-1/4}p^{-1/4}\Psi(z)+\Psi(z)}{\sqrt{\kappa+\eta}}\right),

(7.34)

where we used the control
zm^{\prime}/m\asymp\phi^{1/2}/\sqrt{\kappa+\eta}.
Recall (
7.16
) and let
\Omega
be defined exactly in the same way as
\Omega_{2}^{0}
for
z.
Combining (
7.22
), (
7.23
) and (
7.34
), by a discussion similar to (
7.6
), we readily obtain that

\mathcal{M}_{\eta_{0},E}=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\Omega}\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{z}}\tilde{f}_{i}(z)b(z)\mathrm{d}^{2}z+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n\eta_{0}\sqrt{\kappa+\eta_{0}}}}\right),

where we used Lemma
5.1
. By Lemma
5.9
, we have that

\mathcal{M}_{\eta_{0},E}=\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\int_{\partial\Omega}\tilde{f}_{i}(z)b(z)\mathrm{d}z+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n\eta_{0}\sqrt{\kappa+\eta_{0}}}}\right).

Finally, we can follow lines of the arguments between (
7.17
) and (
7.1
) to conclude the proof of (
(1).
) and (
3.15
).

Distribution of
\mathcal{Z}_{\eta_{0},E}(f_{i})
.
Using Lemma
7.1
, by a discussion similar to the proof of Theorem
6.1
, we can use Lemma
5.11
to conclude that
\{\mathcal{Z}_{\eta_{0},E}(f_{i})\}
follows a multivariate Gaussian distribution asymptotically whose mean is
\mathbf{0}
and covariance matrices satisfy (
3.8
) for
\eta_{0}\asymp 1
and (
3.16
) for
\eta_{0}=\mathrm{o}(1).
This completes the proof of Theorems
3.2
and
3.7
using (
3.17
).

8
Proof of the corollaries

8.1
Proof of Corollary
3.5

In this subsection, we prove Corollary
3.5
using Theorem
3.2
. Note that when
\Sigma=I,
(
2.4
) becomes

z=-\frac{1}{m(z)}+\frac{\phi^{1/2}}{1+\phi^{-1/2}m(z)},

(8.1)

or equivalently,

m(z):=\frac{\phi^{1/2}-\phi^{-1/2}-z+\mathrm{i}\sqrt{\left(z-\gamma_{-}\right)\left(\gamma_{+}-z\right)}}{2\phi^{-1/2}z}.

(8.2)

Proof of Part (1).
Following
[
62
, Section 3]
, for some
a,r
and
\xi,
we consider a change of variable that

m=-\frac{1}{a+r\xi},\ |\xi|=1.

(8.3)

Together with (
8.1
), we see that

\frac{\phi^{1/2}}{1+\phi^{-1/2}m}=\frac{1}{1+\frac{-\phi^{-1/2}}{a+r\xi}}=\frac{\phi^{1/2}(a+r\xi)}{-\phi^{-1/2}+a+r\xi}.

Set
a=\phi^{-1/2}
, we further have

\frac{\phi^{1/2}}{1+\phi^{-1/2}m}=r^{-1}\bar{\xi}+\phi^{1/2}.

This yields that

z=-\frac{1}{m}+\frac{\phi^{1/2}}{1+\phi^{-1/2}m}=\phi^{1/2}+\phi^{-1/2}+r\xi+r^{-1}\bar{\xi},\ \mathrm{d}z=(r-r^{-1}\xi^{-2})\mathrm{d}\xi,

(8.4)

and

\begin{aligned} m^{\prime}(z)=-\frac{m^{2}+\phi^{1/2}m}{2zm+z\phi^{1/2}+1-\phi}=\frac{r^{2}\xi^{2}}{(\phi^{-1/2}+r\xi)^{2}(r^{2}\xi^{2}-1)}\end{aligned}.

Together with the chain rule that
m^{\prime\prime}(z)\mathrm{d}z=\left(\frac{\partial m^{\prime}}{\partial\xi}\right)\mathrm{d}\xi,
it is easy to see that

\frac{m^{\prime\prime}(z)}{2m^{\prime}(z)}\mathrm{d}z=\left[\frac{2}{\xi}-\frac{2r}{\phi^{-1/2}+r\xi}-\frac{2r^{2}\xi}{r^{2}\xi^{2}-1}\right]\mathrm{d}\xi.

(8.5)

We first with the mean part. According to Remark
3.3
and (
3.4
), we decompose that

\mathsf{M}(f)\equiv\mathbb{E}\mathscr{G}:=\mathsf{M}_{1}(f)+\mathsf{M}_{2}(f),

(8.6)

where
\mathsf{M}_{k},k=1,2,
correspond to the
b_{k}
part in (
3.4
). On the one hand, using (
8.4
), (
8.5
) and Remark
3.3
with calculations similar to
[
62
, Section 3.2]
, we see that

\displaystyle\mathsf{M}_{1}(f)=

\displaystyle\frac{-1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\Gamma}f(z)\left(\frac{m^{\prime\prime}(z)}{2m^{\prime}(z)}-\frac{m^{\prime}(z)}{m(z)}\right)\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{-1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{|\xi|=1}f({\phi^{1/2}+\phi^{-1/2}}+r\xi+\frac{1}{r\xi})\left(\frac{1}{\xi}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{\xi+\frac{1}{r}}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{\xi-\frac{1}{r}}\right)\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{r\downarrow 1}\frac{-1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{|\xi|=1}f({\phi^{1/2}+\phi^{-1/2}}+\xi+\frac{1}{\xi})\left(\frac{1}{\xi}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{\xi+\frac{1}{r}}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{\xi-\frac{1}{r}}\right)\mathrm{d}\xi.

(8.7)

On the other hand, for
\mathsf{M}_{2}(f),
with a discussion similar to (
8.5
), we have that

\displaystyle\left[\frac{m^{2}(z)m^{\prime\prime}(z)}{2{m^{\prime}(z)}^{2}}-m(z)\right]\mathrm{d}z=

\displaystyle\left[\frac{1}{\xi}-\frac{1}{r^{2}\xi^{3}}-\frac{1}{\frac{\phi^{-1/2}}{r}+\xi}-\frac{1}{\xi}+\frac{1}{r^{2}\xi^{2}}\frac{1}{\xi+\frac{\phi^{-1/2}}{r}}+\frac{1}{\xi+\frac{\phi^{-1/2}}{r}}-\frac{1}{r^{2}\xi^{2}}\frac{1}{\xi+\frac{\phi^{-1/2}}{r}}\right]\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{r^{2}\xi^{3}}\mathrm{d}\xi.

Together with a discussion similar to the
\mathsf{M}_{1}(f)
part, we see that

\displaystyle\mathsf{M}_{2}(f)

\displaystyle=\kappa_{4}\frac{-1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{|\xi|=1}f({\phi^{1/2}+\phi^{-1/2}}+r\xi+\frac{1}{r\xi})\left(-\frac{1}{r^{2}\xi^{3}}\right)\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle=\kappa_{4}\frac{-1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{|\xi|=1}f({\phi^{1/2}+\phi^{-1/2}}+\xi+\frac{1}{\xi})\left(-\frac{1}{\xi^{3}}\right)\mathrm{d}\xi.

(8.8)

We then work with the variance part. Similar to (
8.6
), we also decompose that

\mathsf{V}(f)\equiv\operatorname{Var}(\mathscr{G}):=\mathsf{V}_{1}(f)+\mathsf{V}_{2}(f).

(8.9)

We again follow the ideas of
[
62
, Section 3]
and consider two non-overlapping contours defined by
z_{j}=\phi^{1/2}+\phi^{-1/2}+r_{j}\xi_{j}+r_{j}^{-1}\bar{\xi}_{j},j=1,2,
with
r_{2}>r_{1}>1
. According to Remark
3.3
and (
8.3
) that
m^{\prime}(z_{j})\mathrm{d}z_{j}=\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial\xi_{j}}m(z_{j})\right)\mathrm{d}\xi_{j}=\frac{r_{j}}{(\phi^{-1/2}+r_{j}\xi_{j})^{2}}\mathrm{d}\xi_{j},
by a discussion similar to (
8.7
), we have that

\displaystyle\mathsf{V}_{1}(f)=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\pi^{2}}\oint_{|\xi_{1}|=1}\oint_{|\xi_{2}|=1}\frac{f(z_{1})f(z_{2})}{(m(z_{1})-m(z_{2}))^{2}}\frac{r_{1}}{(\phi^{-1/2}+r_{1}\xi_{1})^{2}}\frac{r_{2}}{(\phi^{-1/2}+r_{2}\xi_{2})^{2}}\mathrm{d}\xi_{1}\mathrm{d}\xi_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\pi^{2}}\oint_{|\xi_{1}|=1}\oint_{|\xi_{2}|=1}\frac{f(z_{1})f(z_{2})}{(r_{1}\xi_{1}-r_{2}\xi_{2})^{2}}r_{1}r_{2}\mathrm{d}\xi_{1}\mathrm{d}\xi_{2}

(8.10)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\pi^{2}}\oint_{|\xi_{1}|=1}\oint_{|\xi_{2}|=1}\frac{\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}f(\phi^{1/2}+\phi^{-1/2}+{r_{1}}\xi_{1}+\frac{1}{{r_{1}}}\xi_{1}^{-1})f(\phi^{1/2}+\phi^{-1/2}+{r_{2}}\xi_{2}+\frac{1}{{r_{2}}}\xi_{2}^{-1})}{(\xi_{1}-\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}\xi_{2})^{2}}\mathrm{d}\xi_{1}\mathrm{d}\xi_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\pi^{2}}\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}r_{2}>r_{1}\\
r_{1},r_{2}\downarrow 1\end{subarray}}\oint_{|\xi_{1}|=1}\oint_{|\xi_{2}|=1}\frac{f(\phi^{1/2}+\phi^{-1/2}+\xi_{1}+\xi_{1}^{-1})f(\phi^{1/2}+\phi^{-1/2}+\xi_{2}+\xi_{2}^{-1})}{(\xi_{1}-\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}\xi_{2})^{2}}\mathrm{d}\xi_{1}\mathrm{d}\xi_{2}.

Similarly, using Remark
3.3
and the fact

\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{j}}\left(\frac{1}{1+\phi^{-1/2}m(z_{j})}\right)\mathrm{d}z_{j}=\frac{\partial}{\partial\xi_{j}}\left(\frac{1}{1+\phi^{-1/2}m(z_{j})}(\xi_{j})\right)\mathrm{d}\xi_{j}=-\frac{\phi^{-1/2}r_{j}^{-1}}{\xi_{j}^{2}}\mathrm{d}\xi_{j},\quad j=1,2,

we readily obtain that

\displaystyle\mathsf{V}_{2}(f)=

\displaystyle-\frac{\kappa_{4}}{4\pi^{2}}\oint_{|\xi_{1}|=1}f(\phi^{1/2}+\phi^{-1/2}+{r_{1}}\xi_{1}+\frac{1}{{r_{1}}}\xi_{1}^{-1})\frac{r_{1}^{-1}}{\xi_{1}^{2}}\mathrm{d}\xi_{1}\oint_{|\xi_{2}|=1}f(\phi^{1/2}+\phi^{-1/2}+{r_{2}}\xi_{2}+\frac{1}{{r_{2}}}\xi_{2}^{-1})\frac{r_{2}^{-1}}{\xi_{2}^{2}}\mathrm{d}\xi_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\kappa_{4}}{4\pi^{2}}\left(\oint_{|\xi|=1}f(\phi^{1/2}+\phi^{-1/2}+\xi+\xi^{-1})\frac{1}{\xi^{2}}\mathrm{d}\xi\right)^{2}.

(8.11)

This completes the proof of part (1).

Proof of Part (2).
Armed with the results from Part (1), we now proceed to the calculations for the concrete examples. First, for
f_{1}(x)=x-(\phi^{1/2}+\phi^{-1/2}-c)
and its mean part, using (
8.7
) and (
8.1
) with straightforward calculations and Cauchy’s integral theorem, we see that

\displaystyle\mathsf{M}_{1}(f_{1})

\displaystyle=\frac{-1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{|\xi|=1}(c+r\xi+\frac{1}{r\xi})\left(\frac{1}{\xi}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{\xi+\frac{1}{r}}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{\xi-\frac{1}{r}}\right)\mathrm{d}\xi=0,

(8.12)

\displaystyle\mathsf{M}_{2}(f_{1})

\displaystyle=\frac{-1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\kappa_{4}\oint_{|\xi|=1}(c+r\xi+\frac{1}{r\xi})\left(-\frac{1}{r^{2}\xi^{3}}\right)\mathrm{d}\xi=0.

For its variance part, according to (
8.10
), we see that

\displaystyle\mathsf{V}_{1}(f_{1})=-\frac{1}{2\pi^{2}}\oint_{|\xi_{2}|=1}(c+{r_{2}}\xi_{2}+\frac{1}{{r_{2}}}\xi_{2}^{-1})\cdot\oint_{|\xi_{1}|=1}\frac{c+{r_{1}}\xi_{1}+\frac{1}{{r_{1}}}\xi_{1}^{-1}}{(\xi_{1}-\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}\xi_{2})^{2}}\mathrm{d}\xi_{1}\mathrm{d}\xi_{2}.

Moreover, it is easy to see that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{|\xi_{1}|=1}\frac{c+{r_{1}}\xi_{1}+\frac{1}{{r_{1}}}\xi_{1}^{-1}}{(\xi_{1}-\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}\xi_{2})^{2}}\mathrm{d}\xi_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{|\xi_{1}|=1}\frac{c\xi_{1}+{r_{1}}\xi_{1}^{2}+\frac{1}{{r_{1}}}}{\xi_{1}(\xi_{1}-\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}\xi_{2})^{2}}\mathrm{d}\xi_{1}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\left[\oint_{|\xi_{1}|=1}\frac{c}{(\xi_{1}-\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}\xi_{2})^{2}}\mathrm{d}\xi_{1}+\oint_{|\xi_{1}|=1}\frac{{r_{1}}\xi_{1}}{(\xi_{1}-\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}\xi_{2})^{2}}\mathrm{d}\xi_{1}+\oint_{|\xi_{1}|=1}\frac{\frac{1}{{r_{1}}}}{\xi_{1}(\xi_{1}-\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}\xi_{2})^{2}}\mathrm{d}\xi_{1}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{{r_{1}}(\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}})^{2}\xi_{2}^{2}}.

This yields that

\displaystyle\mathsf{V}_{1}(f_{1})=

\displaystyle\frac{2\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}}{2\pi\mathrm{i}(\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}})^{2}r_{1}}\oint_{|\xi_{2}|=1}\frac{c\xi_{2}+{r_{2}}\xi_{2}^{2}+\frac{1}{{r_{2}}}}{\xi_{2}^{3}}\mathrm{d}\xi_{2}={2}.

(8.13)

Similarly, using (
8.1
), we can obtain that

\displaystyle\mathsf{V}_{2}(f_{1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\kappa_{4}}{4\pi^{2}}\oint_{|\xi_{1}|=1}f(\phi^{1/2}+\phi^{-1/2}+{r_{1}}\xi_{1}+\frac{1}{{r_{1}}}\xi_{1}^{-1})\frac{r_{1}^{-1}}{\xi_{1}^{2}}\mathrm{d}\xi_{1}\oint_{|\xi_{2}|=1}f(\phi^{1/2}+\phi^{-1/2}+{r_{2}}\xi_{2}+\frac{1}{{r_{2}}}\xi_{2}^{-1})\frac{r_{2}^{-1}}{\xi_{2}^{2}}\mathrm{d}\xi_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\kappa_{4}.

This concludes the proof for
f_{1}(x).

Second, for
f_{2}(x)=[x-(\phi^{1/2}+\phi^{-1/2}-c)]^{2},
by a discussion similar to (
8.12
) using residual theorem, we see that

\mathsf{M}_{1}(f_{2})=1,\ \mathsf{M}_{2}(f_{2})=\kappa_{4}.

For its variance part, using an argument similar to (
8.13
), it is not hard to see that

\displaystyle\mathsf{V}_{1}(f_{2})

\displaystyle=-\frac{\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}}{2\pi^{2}}\oint_{|\xi_{2}|=1}((c+{r_{2}}\xi_{2}+\frac{1}{{r_{2}}}\xi_{2}^{-1})^{2})\cdot\oint_{|\xi_{1}|=1}\frac{(c+{r_{1}}\xi_{1}+\frac{1}{{r_{1}}}\xi_{1}^{-1})^{2}}{(\xi_{1}-\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}\xi_{2})^{2}}\mathrm{d}\xi_{1}\mathrm{d}\xi_{2}

\displaystyle=2\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}\left[\frac{2c}{r_{1}(\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}})^{2}}{2cr_{2}}+\frac{2}{r_{1}^{2}(\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}})^{3}}{r_{2}^{2}}\right]=8c^{2}+4.

Analogously, we have that

\displaystyle\mathsf{V}_{2}(f_{2})=

\displaystyle-\frac{\kappa_{4}}{4\pi^{2}}\oint_{|\xi_{1}|=1}f(\phi^{1/2}+\phi^{-1/2}+{r_{1}}\xi_{1}+\frac{1}{{r_{1}}}\xi_{1}^{-1})\frac{r_{1}^{-1}}{\xi_{1}^{2}}\mathrm{d}\xi_{1}\oint_{|\xi_{2}|=1}f(\phi^{1/2}+\phi^{-1/2}+{r_{2}}\xi_{2}+\frac{1}{{r_{2}}}\xi_{2}^{-1})\frac{r_{2}^{-1}}{\xi_{2}^{2}}\mathrm{d}\xi_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle 4c^{2}\kappa_{4}}.

This concludes the proof for
f_{2}(x).

Finally, for
f_{3}(x)=\log(x-\phi^{1/2}-\phi^{-1/2}+t+t^{-1}),
for the mean part, we first decompose that

\displaystyle\mathsf{M}_{1}(f_{3})=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{|\xi|=1}\log\left((t+{r}\xi)(1+\frac{1}{{r}t\xi})\right)\left[\frac{1}{\xi}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{\xi+\frac{1}{r}}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{\xi-\frac{1}{r}}\right]\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{|\xi|=1}\left[\log(t+{r}\xi)+\log(1+\frac{1}{{r}t\xi})\right]\left[\frac{1}{\xi}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{\xi+\frac{1}{r}}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{\xi-\frac{1}{r}}\right]\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\mathsf{M}_{11}(f_{3})+\mathsf{M}_{12}(f_{3}),

where

\displaystyle\mathsf{M}_{11}(f_{3})=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{|\xi|=1}\log(t+{r}\xi)\left[\frac{1}{\xi}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{\xi+\frac{1}{r}}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{\xi-\frac{1}{r}}\right]\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left[\log(t)-\frac{1}{2}\log(t-1)-\frac{1}{2}\log(t+1)\right]=\frac{1}{2}\log(1-\frac{1}{t^{2}}),

and

\displaystyle\mathsf{M}_{12}(f_{3})=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{|\xi|=1}\log(1+\frac{1}{{r}t\xi})\left[\frac{1}{\xi}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{\xi+\frac{1}{r}}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{\xi-\frac{1}{r}}\right]\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{|z|=1}\log(1+\frac{z}{{r}t})\left[z-\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{\frac{1}{z}+\frac{1}{r}}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{\frac{1}{z}-\frac{1}{r}}\right]\frac{1}{z^{2}}\mathrm{d}z=0.

This results in
\mathsf{M}_{1}(f_{3})=\frac{1}{2}\log(1-\frac{1}{t^{2}}).
Similarly, one can show that

\displaystyle\mathsf{M}_{2}(f_{3})=-\frac{\kappa_{4}}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{|\xi|=1}\log(t+{r}\xi)\left[-\frac{1}{r^{2}\xi^{3}}\right]\mathrm{d}\xi-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{|\xi|=1}\log(1+\frac{1}{{r}t\xi})\left[-\frac{1}{r^{2}\xi^{3}}\right]\mathrm{d}\xi=-\frac{\kappa_{4}}{2t^{2}}.

For its variance part, using a straightforward calculation with Cauchy’s differentiation formula and residual theorem, we have that

\displaystyle\mathsf{V}_{1}(f_{3})

\displaystyle=2\left[\frac{\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}}{2\pi\mathrm{i}(\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}})^{2}}\oint_{\left|\xi_{2}\right|=1}\frac{\log\left(t+{r_{2}}\xi_{2}\right)}{\xi_{2}\left(\frac{1}{\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}}+{r_{1}}t\xi_{2}\right)}\mathrm{d}\xi_{2}+\frac{\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}}{2\pi\mathrm{i}\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}^{2}}\oint_{\left|\xi_{2}\right|=1}\frac{\log\left(1+\xi_{2}^{-1}/({r_{2}}t)\right)}{\xi_{2}\left(\frac{1}{\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}}+{r_{1}}t\xi_{2}\right)}\mathrm{d}\xi_{2}\right]

\displaystyle=2\left(\log t-\log(t-t^{-1})\right).

Similarly, we have that

\displaystyle\mathsf{V}_{2}(f_{3})=

\displaystyle-\frac{\kappa_{4}}{4\pi^{2}}\oint_{|\xi_{1}|=1}f(\phi^{1/2}+\phi^{-1/2}+{r_{1}}\xi_{1}+\frac{1}{{r_{1}}}\xi_{1}^{-1})\frac{r_{1}^{-1}}{\xi_{1}^{2}}\mathrm{d}\xi_{1}\oint_{|\xi_{2}|=1}f(\phi^{1/2}+\phi^{-1/2}+{r_{2}}\xi_{2}+\frac{1}{{r_{2}}}\xi_{2}^{-1})\frac{r_{2}^{-1}}{\xi_{2}^{2}}\mathrm{d}\xi_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\kappa_{4}/t^{2}.

This completes the proof.

8.2
Proof of Corollary
3.9

As in Section
7.1
, we focus our discussion on a single function. The multiple functions setting can be handled in the same ways as in the end of Section
7.1
.

Simplification of variance.
Similar to (
7.18
), we rewrite

\operatorname{Var}(\mathcal{Z}_{\mathrm{l}})=(\mathcal{K}_{2})_{++}+(\mathcal{K}_{2})_{--}-(\mathcal{K}_{2})_{+-}-(\mathcal{K}_{2})_{-+}.

Here we use
\mathcal{Z}_{\mathrm{l}}\equiv\mathcal{Z}_{\eta_{0},E}
when
\eta_{0}=\mathrm{o}(1).
In view of (
7.19
) and (
7.1
), we can write

\displaystyle(\mathcal{K}_{2})_{++}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\eta_{0}^{2}}{4\pi^{2}}\int\int{g}\left(\tilde{x}_{1}\right){g}\left(\tilde{x}_{2}\right)\widehat{\beta}\left(E+\tilde{x}_{1}{\eta_{0}}+\mathrm{i}\underline{\eta}_{1},E+\tilde{x}_{2}{\eta_{0}}+\mathrm{i}\underline{\eta}_{2}\right)\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}_{1}\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}_{2}

\displaystyle=-\frac{{\eta_{0}}^{2}}{2\pi^{2}}\iint_{1\leq\tilde{y}_{1,2}\leq 2}g\left(\tilde{x}_{1}\right)g\left(\tilde{x}_{2}\right)\left[\frac{m^{\prime}\left(\left(E+\tilde{x}_{1}{\eta_{0}}\right)+\mathrm{i}\underline{\eta}_{1}\right)m^{\prime}\left(\left(E+\tilde{x}_{2}{\eta_{0}}\right)+\mathrm{i}\underline{\eta}_{2}\right)}{\left(m\left(\left(E+\tilde{x}_{1}{\eta_{0}}\right)+\mathrm{i}\underline{\eta}_{1}\right)-m\left(\left(E+\tilde{x}_{2}{\eta_{0}}\right)+\mathrm{i}\underline{\eta}_{2}\right)\right)^{2}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\frac{1}{\left(\left(\tilde{x}_{1}-\tilde{x}_{2}\right){\eta_{0}}+\mathrm{i}\frac{1}{2}{\eta_{0}}n^{-\varepsilon_{1}}\right)^{2}}\right]\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}_{1}\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}_{2}

\displaystyle:=\left(\mathcal{K}_{2,1}\right)_{++}+\left(\mathcal{K}_{2,2}\right)_{++},

(8.14)

where we used the definition of
\widehat{\beta}
as in (
3.6
).

We first reformulate the case in the bulk
E\in\left(\gamma_{-}+\tau^{\prime},\gamma_{+}-\tau^{\prime}\right)
for some fixed small constant
\tau^{\prime}>0
. Note that
m
is holomorphic in
\mathbb{C}\backslash[\gamma_{-},\gamma_{+}]
. Since we are dealing with
z_{1},z_{2}
in the same upper half plane, we can use the taylor expansion at
z_{1}
that

\displaystyle\frac{m^{\prime}(z_{1})m^{\prime}(z_{2})}{(m(z_{1})-m(z_{2}))^{2}}-\frac{1}{(z_{1}-z_{2})^{2}}=

\displaystyle\frac{m^{\prime}(z_{1})(m^{\prime}(z_{1})+(z_{2}-z_{1})m^{\prime\prime}(z_{1})+\mathrm{O}(|z_{1}-z_{2}|^{2}))}{((z_{2}-z_{1})m^{\prime}(z_{1})+\mathrm{O}(|z_{1}-z_{2}|^{2}))^{2}}-\frac{1}{(z_{1}-z_{2})^{2}}

(8.15)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{m^{\prime\prime}(z_{1})}{(z_{2}-z_{1})m^{\prime}(z_{1})+\mathrm{O}(|z_{1}-z_{2}|^{2})}+\mathrm{O}(1).

Similarly we have

\displaystyle\frac{m^{\prime}(z_{1})m^{\prime}(z_{2})}{(m(z_{1})-m(z_{2}))^{2}}-\frac{1}{(z_{1}-z_{2})^{2}}=

\displaystyle\frac{m^{\prime}(z_{2})(m^{\prime}(z_{2})+(z_{1}-z_{2})m^{\prime\prime}(z_{2})+\mathrm{O}(|z_{1}-z_{2}|^{2}))}{((z_{1}-z_{2})m^{\prime}(z_{2})+\mathrm{O}(|z_{1}-z_{2}|^{2}))^{2}}-\frac{1}{(z_{1}-z_{2})^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{m^{\prime\prime}(z_{2})}{(z_{1}-z_{2})m^{\prime}(z_{2})+\mathrm{O}(|z_{1}-z_{2}|^{2})}+\mathrm{O}(1).

This implies that

\widehat{\beta}(z_{1},z_{2})\sim\frac{1}{z_{2}-z_{1}}\left(\frac{m^{\prime\prime}(z_{1})}{m^{\prime}(z_{1})}-\frac{m^{\prime\prime}(z_{2})}{m^{\prime}(z_{2})}\right).

(8.16)

Using the above control, we can conclude that

{\eta_{0}}^{2}\left[\frac{m^{\prime}\left(\left(E+\tilde{x}_{1}{\eta_{0}}\right)+\mathrm{i}\underline{\eta}_{1}\right)m^{\prime}\left(\left(E+\tilde{x}_{2}{\eta_{0}}\right)+\mathrm{i}\underline{\eta}_{2}\right)}{\left(m\left(\left(E+\tilde{x}_{1}{\eta_{0}}\right)+\mathrm{i}\underline{\eta}_{1}\right)-m\left(\left(E+\tilde{x}_{2}{\eta_{0}}\right)+\mathrm{i}\underline{\eta}_{2}\right)\right)^{2}}-\frac{1}{\left(\left(\tilde{x}_{1}-\tilde{x}_{2}\right){\eta_{0}}+\mathrm{i}\frac{1}{2}{\eta_{0}}n^{-\varepsilon_{1}}\right)^{2}}\right]=\mathrm{O}({\eta_{0}}^{2}).

This shows that
(\mathcal{K}_{2})_{++}
is negligible and similar discussions and results apply to
(\mathcal{K}_{2})_{--}.

When
z_{1}
and
z_{2}
are in different half planes, we have that

\left|m^{\prime}\left(\left(E+\tilde{x}_{1}{\eta_{0}}\right)+\mathrm{i}\underline{\eta}_{1}\right)\right|,\left|m^{\prime}\left(\left(E+\tilde{x}_{2}{\eta_{0}}\right)+\mathrm{i}\underline{\eta}_{2}\right)\right|\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{\tau^{\prime}}},

and

\left|\left(m\left(\left(E+\tilde{x}_{1}{\eta_{0}}\right)+\mathrm{i}\underline{\eta}_{1}\right)-m\left(\left(E+\tilde{x}_{2}{\eta_{0}}\right)+\mathrm{i}\underline{\eta}_{2}\right)\right)\right|\sim\rho(E+\tilde{x}_{1}\eta_{0})+\rho(E+\tilde{x}_{2}\eta_{0})\sim 1.

We can therefore conclude that
(\mathcal{K}_{2,1})_{+-}=\mathrm{O}(\eta_{0}^{2}/\tau^{\prime})
and
(\mathcal{K}_{2,1})_{-+}=\mathrm{O}(\eta_{0}^{2}/\tau^{\prime}).
For the remaining term
(\mathcal{K}_{2,2})_{+-}+(\mathcal{K}_{2,2})_{-+}
, we have

-\frac{1}{\pi^{2}}\iint g\left(\tilde{x}_{1}\right)g\left(\tilde{x}_{2}\right)\frac{1}{\left(\tilde{x}_{1}-\tilde{x}_{2}\pm\mathrm{i}\frac{3}{2}\eta_{0}n^{-\varepsilon_{1}}\right)^{2}}\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}_{1}\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}_{2}.

Note that we can add additional
\frac{1}{2\pi^{2}}\iint\frac{g\left(\tilde{x}_{1}\right)^{2}}{\left(\tilde{x}_{1}-\tilde{x}_{2}\pm\mathrm{i}\frac{3}{2}n^{-\varepsilon_{1}}\right)^{2}}\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}_{1}\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}_{2}
and
\frac{1}{2\pi^{2}}\iint\frac{g\left(\tilde{x}_{2}\right)^{2}}{\left(\tilde{x}_{1}-\tilde{x}_{2}\pm\mathrm{i}\frac{3}{2}n^{-\varepsilon_{1}}\right)^{2}}\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}_{1}\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}_{2}
to the above terms since they are negligible. Then we can conclude the proof using dominated convergence theorem.

Then we reformulate the case at the edge. Due to similarity, we focus on the right edge
\gamma_{+}.
Note that near the edge, for some constants
c_{1},c_{2}
and
c_{3}=c_{1}/2,
we have the following approximation

m(z)=m(\gamma_{+})+c_{1}\sqrt{z-\gamma_{+}}+\mathrm{O}\left(|z-\gamma_{+}|\right),\ m^{\prime}(z)=c_{2}+\frac{c_{3}}{\sqrt{z-\gamma_{+}}}+\mathrm{O}(\sqrt{|z-\gamma_{+}|}).

(8.17)

For the terms in (
8.2
) and counterparts in
(\mathcal{K}_{2})_{--},(\mathcal{K}_{2})_{-+}
and
(\mathcal{K}_{2})_{+-}
, it is not hard to see that

(\mathcal{K}_{2,2})_{++}+(\mathcal{K}_{2,2})_{--}+(\mathcal{K}_{2,2})_{+-}+(\mathcal{K}_{2,2})_{-+}=0.

Then it suffices to control
(\mathcal{K}_{2,1})_{++},(\mathcal{K}_{2,1})_{--},(\mathcal{K}_{2,1})_{-+}
and
(\mathcal{K}_{2,1})_{+-}.
Note that

\displaystyle(\mathcal{K}_{2,1})_{++}=-\frac{1}{8\pi^{2}}\iint\frac{g(\tilde{x_{1}})g(\tilde{x}_{2})\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\tilde{x}_{1}+\mathrm{i}n^{-\varepsilon_{1}}}}+\mathrm{O}(\sqrt{{\eta_{0}}})\right)\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\tilde{x}_{2}+\mathrm{i}n^{-\varepsilon_{1}}/2}}+\mathrm{O}(\sqrt{{\eta_{0}}})\right)}{\left(\sqrt{\tilde{x}_{1}+\mathrm{i}n^{-\varepsilon_{1}}}-\sqrt{\tilde{x}_{2}+\mathrm{i}n^{-\varepsilon_{1}}/2}+\mathrm{O}(\sqrt{{\eta_{0}}})\right)^{2}}\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}_{1}\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}_{2}
