\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{8\pi^{2}}\iint\frac{g(\tilde{x_{1}})g(\tilde{x}_{2})}{\sqrt{\tilde{x}_{1}+\mathrm{i}n^{-\varepsilon_{1}}}\sqrt{\tilde{x}_{2}+\mathrm{i}n^{-\varepsilon_{1}}/2}\left(\sqrt{\tilde{x}_{1}+\mathrm{i}n^{-\varepsilon_{1}}}-\sqrt{\tilde{x}_{2}+\mathrm{i}n^{-\varepsilon_{1}}}\right)^{2}}\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}_{1}\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}_{2}+\mathrm{O}(\sqrt{{\eta_{0}}}n^{3\varepsilon_{1}}).

Similar to the discussion in the bulk, we could add vanishing
g(\tilde{x}_{1})^{2}
and
g(\tilde{x}_{1})^{2}
terms by consider the corresponding complex integral and use dominated convergence theorem to show that

\displaystyle(\mathcal{K}_{2,1})_{++}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{16\pi^{2}}\iint\frac{(g(\tilde{x}_{1})-g(\tilde{x}_{2}))^{2}}{\sqrt{\tilde{x_{1}}+\mathrm{i}0}\sqrt{\tilde{x}_{2}+\mathrm{i}0}(\sqrt{\tilde{x_{1}}+\mathrm{i}0}-\sqrt{\tilde{x_{2}}+\mathrm{i}0})^{2}}\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}_{1}\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}_{2}+\mathrm{o}(1)

(8.18)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4\pi^{2}}\int_{\psi(\mathbb{R}+\mathrm{i}0)}\int_{\psi(\mathbb{R}+\mathrm{i}0)}\frac{\left(g\left(w_{1}^{2}\right)-g\left(w_{2}^{2}\right)\right)^{2}}{\left(w_{1}-w_{2}\right)^{2}}\mathrm{~d}w_{1}\mathrm{~d}w_{2}+\mathrm{o}(1),

where we used the change the variable
\psi(z):=\sqrt{z}
with branch cut such that
\psi:\mathbb{C}^{+}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{+}
. Similarly, we also have

\displaystyle(\mathcal{K}_{2,1})_{--}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4\pi^{2}}\int_{\psi(\mathbb{R}-\mathrm{i}0)}\int_{\psi(\mathbb{R}-\mathrm{i}0)}\frac{\left(g\left(w_{1}^{2}\right)-g\left(w_{2}^{2}\right)\right)^{2}}{\left(w_{1}-w_{2}\right)^{2}}\mathrm{d}w_{1}\mathrm{d}w_{2}+\mathrm{o}(1),

\displaystyle(\mathcal{K}_{2,1})_{+-}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4\pi^{2}}\int_{\psi(\mathbb{R}+\mathrm{i}0)}\int_{\psi(\mathbb{R}-\mathrm{i}0)}\frac{\left(g\left(w_{1}^{2}\right)-g\left(w_{2}^{2}\right)\right)^{2}}{\left(w_{1}-w_{2}\right)^{2}}\mathrm{d}w_{1}\mathrm{d}w_{2}+\mathrm{o}(1),

\displaystyle(\mathcal{K}_{2,1})_{-+}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4\pi^{2}}\int_{\psi(\mathbb{R}-\mathrm{i}0)}\int_{\psi(\mathbb{R}+\mathrm{i}0)}\frac{\left(g\left(w_{1}^{2}\right)-g\left(w_{2}^{2}\right)\right)^{2}}{\left(w_{1}-w_{2}\right)^{2}}\mathrm{d}w_{1}\mathrm{d}w_{2}+\mathrm{o}(1).

This yields that

\mathcal{K}_{2,1}=\frac{1}{4\pi^{2}}\int_{-\mathrm{i}\infty}^{\mathrm{i}\infty}\int_{-\mathrm{i}\infty}^{\mathrm{i}\infty}\frac{\left(g\left(w_{1}^{2}\right)-g\left(w_{2}^{2}\right)\right)^{2}}{\left(w_{1}-w_{2}\right)^{2}}\mathrm{d}w_{1}\mathrm{d}w_{2}+\mathrm{o}(1)=\frac{1}{4\pi^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\left(g\left(-w_{1}^{2}\right)-g\left(-w_{2}^{2}\right)\right)^{2}}{\left(w_{1}-w_{2}\right)^{2}}\mathrm{d}w_{1}\mathrm{d}w_{2}+\mathrm{o}(1).

Simplification of mean.
When
\operatorname{Re}z
is in the bulk, we have that
|m(z)|\sim 1,\ {m}^{\prime}(z)\sim(\kappa+{\eta})^{-1/2}.
Using the change of variable as in (
7.20
) and a discussion similar to (
7.1
), we have that

\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}f(x)b_{1}^{\mathfrak{+}}(x)\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\mathbb{R}}f(x)b_{1}^{\mathfrak{-}}(x)\mathrm{d}x\right)=\frac{\eta_{0}}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}g(\tilde{x})b_{1}^{\mathfrak{+}}(E+\tilde{x}\eta_{0})\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}-\int_{\mathbb{R}}g(\tilde{x})b_{1}^{\mathfrak{-}}(E+\tilde{x}\eta_{0})\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}\right).

Furthermore, by definition and Lemma
5.1
, we have that
b_{1}(x)=\mathrm{O}\left(1+n^{\varepsilon_{1}/2}\eta_{0}^{-1/2}\right).
This shows that the mean is asymptotically
0
in the bulk.

Next, at the edge, using (
8.17
) and

m^{\prime\prime}(z)=-\frac{c_{3}}{2\left(\sqrt{z-\gamma_{+}}\right)^{3}}+\mathrm{O}\left(\left|z-\gamma_{+}\right|^{-1/2}\right).

Consequently, we have that

\left(\frac{m^{\prime\prime}(z)}{2m^{\prime}(z)}-\frac{m^{\prime}(z)}{m(z)}\right)+\kappa_{4}\left(\frac{m^{2}(z)m^{\prime\prime}(z)}{2({m^{\prime}}(z))^{2}}-m(z)\right)=\frac{1}{4(z-\gamma_{+})}+\mathrm{O}\left(\left|z-\gamma_{+}\right|^{-1/2}\right).

Inserting the above estimate into (
3.15
), by Lemma
5.10
, we have that the mean is asymptotically
g(0)/4.

8.3
Proof of Corollary
4.2

For the global statistics, (
4.2
) and (
4.4
) follow directly from (2) of Corollary
3.5
and (
3.14
). For (
4.3
), we can prove it using Delta method, i.e., if
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}(\mathbf{a})
exists and
n^{b}\left(\bm{x}_{n}-\mathbf{a}\right)\Rightarrow\bm{x}
for some fixed constant
b>0
, then
n^{b}\left(\mathfrak{g}\left(\bm{x}_{n}\right)-\mathfrak{g}(\mathbf{a})\right)\Rightarrow[\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}(\mathbf{a})]^{*}\bm{x}.
In light of (
4.2
) and the definition of
\mathcal{T}_{4}^{\mathrm{g}},
we set

\mathfrak{g}(y_{1},y_{2})=\frac{y_{2}}{y_{1}^{2}},\ \bm{x}_{n}=(\mathcal{T}_{1}^{\mathrm{g}},\mathcal{T}_{2}^{\mathrm{g}})^{\prime}.

Therefore, in order to apply Delta method, it suffices to find the distribution of
\bm{x}_{n}
using Theorem
3.2
under
\Sigma=I.
Recall (
8.9
). Following lines of the arguments in Section
8.1
, we have that

\displaystyle\mathsf{V}_{1}(f_{1},f_{2})=

\displaystyle 4c,\quad\mathsf{V}_{2}(f_{1},f_{2})=2c\kappa_{4},

so that
\mathsf{V}(f_{1},f_{2})=4c+2c\kappa_{4}.
Then together with Theorem
3.2
and (2) of Corollary
3.5
, we see from (
4.2
) that the distribution of
\bm{x}_{n}
follows

\begin{pmatrix}\mathcal{T}_{1}^{\mathrm{g}}-n(c-\phi^{-1/2})\\
\mathcal{T}_{2}^{\mathrm{g}}-n(1+c^{2}-2c\phi^{-1/2}+\phi^{-1})\end{pmatrix}\Rightarrow\mathcal{N}_{2}\left(\begin{pmatrix}0\\
1+\kappa_{4}\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}2+\kappa_{4}&4c+2c\kappa_{4}\\
4c+2c\kappa_{4}&4+4c^{2}(\kappa_{4}+2)\end{pmatrix}\right).

Then the proof follows from Delta method with straightforward calculations.

For the local statistics, (
4.5
) follows directly from (2) of Corollary
3.9
. The proof of (
4.6
) follows from the Delta method as for (
4.3
). This completes the proof.

8.4
Proof of Corollary
4.3

Note that (
3.1
), the centering depends on
\Sigma
under
\mathbf{H}_{a}
in (
4.7
) so that the definitions in (
3.2
) need to be modified accordingly. In the proof, we focus on a single test function and let
f_{0}
be defined as in (
3.2
) under
\mathbf{H}_{0}
and
f_{a}
be that under
\mathbf{H}_{a}.
Moreover, under
\mathbf{H}_{a},
we denote

\mathrm{m}_{\mathfrak{d}}:=\mathbb{E}\left(Z_{\eta_{0},E}(f_{\mathfrak{d}})\right),\ \mathrm{v}_{\mathfrak{d}}=\operatorname{Var}\left(Z_{\eta_{0},E}(f_{\mathfrak{d}})\right),\ \mathfrak{d}=0,\ a.

We also denote
\mathrm{m}^{\prime}:=\mathbb{E}\left(Z_{\eta_{0},E}(f_{0})\right)
under
\mathbf{H}_{0}.

Proof.

Note that under
\mathbf{H}_{a},
we have that

\frac{Z_{\eta_{0},E}(f_{a})-\mathrm{m}_{a}}{\sqrt{\mathrm{v}_{a}}}\Rightarrow\mathcal{N}(0,1).

Moreover, under the nominal level
\alpha,
we shall reject
\mathbf{H}_{a}
if

\frac{Z_{\eta_{0},E}(f_{0})-\mathrm{m}_{0}}{\sqrt{\mathrm{v}_{0}}}>z_{1-\alpha/2}\ \text{or}\ \frac{Z_{\eta_{0},E}(f_{0})-\mathrm{m}_{0}}{\sqrt{\mathrm{v}_{0}}}<z_{\alpha/2}.

Due to similarity, we focus on the left tail and the power can be written as

\mathsf{Po}:=\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{Z_{\eta_{0},E}(f_{0})-\mathrm{m}_{0}}{\sqrt{\mathrm{v}_{0}}}<z_{\alpha/2}|\mathbf{H}_{a}\right).

After a straightforward calculation, we can rewrite

\mathsf{Po}=\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{Z_{\eta_{0},E}(f_{a})-\mathrm{m}_{a}}{\sqrt{\mathrm{v}_{a}}}<T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}+T_{4}|\mathbf{H}_{a}\right),

where
T_{i},1\leq i\leq 4
are defined as

T_{1}:=z_{\alpha/2}\frac{\sqrt{\mathrm{v}_{0}}}{\sqrt{\mathrm{v}_{a}}},\ T_{2}:=\frac{\mathrm{m}^{\prime}-\mathrm{m}_{0}}{\sqrt{\mathrm{v}_{a}}},\ T_{3}:=-\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}(f_{0}(\lambda_{i})-f_{a}(\lambda_{i}))+n(\int f_{a}\mathrm{d}\varrho_{a}-\int f_{0}\mathrm{d}\varrho_{a})+\mathrm{m}_{a}-\mathrm{m}_{0}}{\sqrt{\mathrm{v}_{a}}},

T_{4}:=-\frac{n(\int f_{0}\mathrm{d}\varrho_{a}-\int f_{0}\mathrm{d}\varrho_{0})}{\sqrt{\mathrm{v}_{a}}}.

It is not hard to see that
T_{i}=\mathrm{O}(1),1\leq i\leq 3.
Consequently, to have
\mathsf{Po}\rightarrow 1,
we require
T_{4}\rightarrow\infty
for various test functions. Due to similarity, we only focus on the setting when
\mathsf{sy}=\mathrm{g}
and
\ell=1,2.

When
\ell=1,
if
(\mathfrak{m}_{1}(\pi_{a})-1)\sqrt{\phi}\gg 1,
we a=have that
\int f_{0}\mathrm{d}\varrho_{a}-\int f_{0}\mathrm{d}\varrho_{0}=\int(f_{0}-f_{a})\mathrm{d}\varrho_{a}+(\int f_{a}\mathrm{d}\varrho_{a}-\int f_{0}\mathrm{d}\varrho_{0})=(\mathfrak{m}_{1}(\pi_{a})-1)\sqrt{\phi}+\mathrm{O}(1).
This yields that

T_{4}\asymp n\phi^{1/2}(\mathfrak{m}_{1}(\pi_{a})-1).

Furthermore,
(\mathfrak{m}_{1}(\pi_{a})-1)\sqrt{\phi}=\mathrm{O}(1),
we still have
\int f_{0}\mathrm{d}\varrho_{a}-\int f_{0}\mathrm{d}\varrho_{0}=\int(f_{0}-f_{a})\mathrm{d}\varrho_{a}+(\int f_{a}\mathrm{d}\varrho_{a}-\int f_{0}\mathrm{d}\varrho_{0})=(\mathfrak{m}_{1}(\pi_{a})-1)\sqrt{\phi}+\mathrm{O}((\mathfrak{m}_{1}(\pi_{a})-1)).
This verifies (
4.9
) for
\ell=1
.

When
\ell=2,
we decompose that
\int f_{0}\mathrm{d}\varrho_{a}-\int f_{0}\mathrm{d}\varrho_{0}=\int(f_{0}-f_{a})\mathrm{d}\varrho_{a}+(\int f_{a}\mathrm{d}\varrho_{a}-\int f_{0}\mathrm{d}\varrho_{0}).
Using

\mathrm{d}\varrho_{a}(x)=\left(\frac{\sqrt{4\mathfrak{m}_{2}(\pi_{a})^{2}-(x-\mathfrak{m}_{1}(\pi_{a})\sqrt{\phi})^{2}}}{2\pi\mathfrak{m}_{2}(\pi_{a})}+\mathrm{O}(1/\sqrt{\phi})\right)\mathrm{d}x,

we have that

\int x\mathrm{d}\varrho_{a}=\mathfrak{m}_{1}(\pi_{a})\sqrt{\phi}+\mathrm{O}(\mathfrak{m}_{1}(\pi_{a})),

\int x^{2}\mathrm{d}\varrho_{a}=\mathfrak{m}_{2}(\pi_{a})^{2}+2(\mathfrak{m}_{1}(\pi_{a})\sqrt{\phi})^{2}-(\mathfrak{m}_{1}(\pi_{a})\sqrt{\phi})^{2}+\mathrm{O}(\mathfrak{m}_{1}(\pi_{a})^{2}\sqrt{\phi})=\mathfrak{m}_{2}(\pi_{a})^{2}+\mathfrak{m}_{1}(\pi_{a})^{2}\phi+\mathrm{O}(\mathfrak{m}_{1}(\pi_{a})^{2}\sqrt{\phi}),

\displaystyle\int(x-\sqrt{\phi}-1/\sqrt{\phi}+c)^{2}\mathrm{d}\varrho_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}_{1}(\pi_{a})^{2}\phi-2\sqrt{\phi}\mathfrak{m}_{1}(\pi_{a})\sqrt{\phi}+\phi+(c-1/\sqrt{\phi})^{2}+{\mathfrak{m}_{2}(\pi_{a})^{2}}+\mathrm{O}((\mathfrak{m}_{1}(\pi_{a})-1)^{2}\sqrt{\phi})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\mathfrak{m}_{1}(\pi_{a})-1)^{2}\phi+(c-1/\sqrt{\phi})^{2}+{\mathfrak{m}_{2}(\pi_{a})^{2}}+2c(\mathfrak{m}_{1}(\pi_{a})-1)\sqrt{\phi}+\mathrm{O}((\mathfrak{m}_{1}(\pi_{a})-1)^{2}\sqrt{\phi}).

Meanwhile, we have for
\ell=2,

\int f_{0}\mathrm{d}\varrho_{0}=1+c^{2}-2c\phi^{-1/2}+\phi^{-1}.

Combining all the above controls, we have that

\displaystyle T_{4}\asymp n\left((\mathfrak{m}_{1}(\pi_{a})-1)^{2}{\phi}+2c(\mathfrak{m}_{1}(\pi_{a})-1)\sqrt{\phi}+(\mathfrak{m}_{2}(\pi_{a})^{2}-1)+\mathrm{O}((\mathfrak{m}_{1}(\pi_{a})-1)^{2}\sqrt{\phi})\right).

This verifies (
4.10
) for
\ell=2.
The other cases can be handled similarly. This completes our proof.

∎

Appendix A
Proof of Theorem
5.5

In this section, we prove the main ingredient, Theorem
5.5
following
[
14
,
35
]
with necessary modifications. In Section
A.1
, we summarize the basic tools. In Section
A.2
, we prove the entrywise local law when
\mathbf{u}_{k},\mathbf{v}_{k}
in (
5.14
) and (
5.15
) are standard basis. We also prove (
5.16
), (
5.17
) and (
5.18
). In Section
A.3
, armed with the entrywise local law, we prove (
5.14
) and (
5.15
) under the general setting.

A.1
Some tools

In this section, we provide some technical preparation. Recall (
5.12
). We denote

G_{1}(z):=z\Sigma^{1/2}R_{1}(z)\Sigma^{1/2},\ G_{2}(z):=R_{2}(z).

(A.1)

It is not hard to see that by Schur’s complement,

G=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\Sigma XG_{2}X^{*}\Sigma-\Sigma&\Sigma XG_{2}\\
G_{2}X^{*}\Sigma&G_{2}\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}G_{1}&z^{-1}G_{1}X\\
z^{-1}X^{*}G_{1}&z^{-2}X^{*}G_{1}X-z^{-1}\end{array}\right).

(A.2)

Following Definition 4.1 of
[
35
]
, for matrices of the form
A=(A_{st}:s\in l(A),t\in r(A)),
whose entries are indexed by arbitrary finite subsets of
l(A),r(A)\subset\mathbb{N},
then the matrix multiplication
AB
is defined for
s\in l(A)
and
t\in r(B)
by

(AB)_{st}:=\sum_{q\in l(A)\cap r(B)}A_{sq}B_{qt}.

Recall (
5.10
). Let
H\equiv H(z):=G^{-1}(z).
For
S\subset\mathcal{I}
we define the minor
H^{(S)}:=\left(H_{st}:s,t\in\mathcal{I}\backslash S\right).
Note that
H^{(S)}
is also an
(p+n)\times(p+n)
matrix where the undefined entries are zeros. We also write
G^{(S)}:=\left(H^{(S)}\right)^{-1}.
The matrices
G_{1}^{(S)}
and
G_{2}^{(S)}
can be defined similarly. Throughout the paper, we abbreviate
(\{s\})\equiv(s)
and
(\{s,t\})\equiv(st)
. The following identities will be frequently used in our calculations.

Lemma A.1
.

Recall Definition
5.3
, (
5.10
), (
A.1
) and the assumption that
\Sigma
is diagonal. We have that

(1).

For
\mu\in\mathcal{I}_{2}
we have

\frac{1}{G_{\mu\mu}}=-z-\left(X^{*}G^{(\mu)}X\right)_{\mu\mu},

and for
\mu\neq\nu\in\mathcal{I}_{2}

G_{\mu\nu}=-G_{\mu\mu}\left(X^{*}G^{(\mu)}\right)_{\mu\nu}=-G_{\nu\nu}\left(G^{(\nu)}X\right)_{\mu\nu}=G_{\mu\mu}G_{\nu\nu}^{(\mu)}\left(X^{*}G^{(\mu\nu)}X\right)_{\mu\nu}.

(2).

For
i\in\mathcal{I}_{1}
we have

\frac{1}{G_{ii}}=-\frac{1}{\sigma_{i}}-\left(XG^{(i)}X^{*}\right)_{ii},

(A.3)

and for
i\neq j\in\mathcal{I}_{1}

G_{ij}=-G_{ii}\left(XG^{(i)}\right)_{ij}=-G_{jj}\left(G^{(j)}X^{*}\right)_{ij}=G_{ii}G_{jj}^{(i)}\left(XG^{(ij)}X^{*}\right)_{ij}.

(A.4)

(3).

For
i\in\mathcal{I}_{1}
and
\mu\in\mathcal{I}_{2}
we have,

G_{i\mu}=-G_{\mu\mu}\left(G^{(\mu)}X\right)_{i\mu},\quad G_{\mu i}=-G_{\mu\mu}\left(X^{*}G^{(\mu)}\right)_{\mu i},

and

\displaystyle G_{i\mu}=-G_{ii}\left(XG^{(i)}\right)_{i\mu}=G_{ii}G_{\mu\mu}^{(i)}\left(-x_{i\mu}+\left(XG^{(i\mu)}X\right)_{i\mu}\right)

\displaystyle G_{\mu i}=-G_{ii}\left(G^{(i)}X\right)_{\mu i}=G_{\mu\mu}G_{ii}^{(\mu)}\left(-x_{\mu i}+\left(X^{*}G^{(\mu i)}X^{*}\right)_{\mu i}\right).

(4).

For
r\in\mathcal{I}
and
s,t\in\mathcal{I}\backslash\{r\}
we have

G_{st}^{(r)}=G_{st}-\frac{G_{sr}G_{rt}}{G_{rr}},\quad\frac{1}{G_{ss}}=\frac{1}{G_{ss}^{(r)}}-\frac{G_{sr}G_{rs}}{G_{ss}G_{ss}^{(r)}G_{rr}}.

(A.5)

(5).

All of the identities from (1)-(4) hold for
G^{(S)}
if
S\subset\mathcal{I}_{2}
or
S\subset\mathcal{I}.

Proof.

See Lemma 4.4 of
[
35
]
.
∎

Next, we introduce the spectral decomposition of
G
. Denote the singular value decomposition of
\Sigma^{1/2}X
as
\Sigma^{1/2}X=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sqrt{\lambda_{k}}\bm{\xi}_{k}\bm{\zeta}_{k}^{*},
where
\sqrt{\lambda_{1}}\geqslant\sqrt{\lambda_{2}}\geqslant\cdots\geqslant\sqrt{\lambda_{n}}>0
are the singular values and
\left\{\bm{\xi}_{k}\right\}
and
\left\{\bm{\zeta}_{k}\right\}
the associated singular vectors. Then according to (
A.2
), for
\mu,\nu\in\mathcal{I}_{2},
we have

G_{\mu\nu}(z)=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\frac{\bm{\zeta}_{k}(\mu)\overline{\bm{\zeta}_{k}(\nu)}}{\lambda_{k}-z}.

(A.6)

Similarly, by defining

\mathbf{u}_{k}:=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbf{1}(k\leqslant p)\sqrt{\lambda_{k}}\bm{\xi}_{k}\\
\mathbf{1}(k\leqslant n)\zeta_{k}\end{array}\right)\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathcal{I}},\ \overline{\Sigma}:=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\Sigma&0\\
0&I_{n}\end{array}\right)\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathcal{I}\times\mathcal{I}},

we have (see equation (4.15) of
[
35
]
)

G=-\overline{\Sigma}+\overline{\Sigma}^{1/2}\sum_{k=1}^{p}\frac{\mathbf{u}_{k}\mathbf{u}_{k}^{*}}{\lambda_{k}-z}\overline{\Sigma}^{1/2}.

(A.7)

Next, we provide some preliminary controls for the resolvent
G.
For the deterministic vectors
\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathcal{I}_{1}}
and
\mathbf{w}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathcal{I}_{2}},
we identity them with their natural embeddings
(\mathbf{v}\ \ 0)^{*}
and
(0\ \ \mathbf{w})^{*}
in
\mathbb{R}^{\mathcal{I}}.
Moreover, for an
\mathcal{I}\times\mathcal{I}
matrix
A,
we use the following notations for their generalized entries

A_{\mathbf{vw}}:=\mathbf{v}^{*}A\mathbf{w},\quad A_{\mathbf{v}s}:=\mathbf{v}^{*}A\mathbf{e}_{s},\quad A_{s\mathbf{v}}:=\mathbf{e}_{s}^{*}A\mathbf{v},

where
\mathbf{e}_{s}
denotes the standard unit vector in the coordinate direction
s
in
\mathbb{R}^{\mathcal{I}}
. The following deterministic controls will be frequently used in our proofs.

Lemma A.2
.

For
z\in\mathbf{S}
uniformly in (
5.1
), we have that for some constant
C>0,
with high probability

\left\|\overline{\Sigma}^{-1/2}G\overline{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\right\|\leqslant C{(\phi^{1/2}+\phi^{-1/2})}\eta^{-1},\quad\left\|\overline{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\partial_{z}G\overline{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\right\|\leqslant C{(\phi^{1/2}+\phi^{-1/2})}\eta^{-2}.

(A.8)

Furthermore, for unit vectors
\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathcal{I}_{1}}
and
\mathbf{w}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathcal{I}_{2}}
, then for some constant
C>0

\sum_{\mu\in\mathcal{I}_{2}}\left|G_{\mathbf{w}\mu}\right|^{2}=\frac{\operatorname{Im}G_{\mathbf{w}\mathbf{w}}}{\eta},\\

(A.9)

\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{1}}\left|G_{\mathbf{v}i}\right|^{2}\leqslant\frac{C(\phi^{1/2}+\phi^{-1/2})}{\eta}\operatorname{Im}G_{\mathbf{v}\mathbf{v}}+2\left(\Sigma^{2}\right)_{\mathbf{v}\mathbf{v}},\\

(A.10)

\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{1}}\left|G_{\mathbf{w}i}\right|^{2}\leqslant C(\phi^{1/2}+\phi^{-1/2})\sum_{\mu\in\mathcal{I}_{N}}\left|G_{\mathbf{w}\mu}\right|^{2},\\

(A.11)

\sum_{\mu\in\mathcal{I}_{2}}\left|G_{\mathbf{v}\mu}\right|^{2}\leqslant C(\phi^{1/2}+\phi^{-1/2})\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{1}}\left|G_{\mathbf{v}i}\right|^{2}.

(A.12)

Finally, the estimates (
A.9
)-(
A.12
) remain true for
G^{(S)}
if
S\subset\mathcal{I}_{2}
or
S\subset\mathcal{I}
.

Proof.

Note that according to
[
5
]
and the scaling (
2.1
), we find that with high probability, for some constant
C>0,

\left\|X^{*}X\right\|\leq C(\phi^{1/2}+\phi^{-1/2}).

The estimates (
A.8
) follow from (
A.7
), using
\|A\|=\sup\{|\langle\mathbf{x},A\mathbf{y}\rangle|:|\mathbf{x}|,|\mathbf{y}|\leqslant 1\}
, and since we are in the spectral domain
\mathbf{S}
so
|\operatorname{Re}z-\lambda_{k}|\asymp 1
,
\left|\lambda_{k}\right|/\left|\lambda_{k}-z\right|\leqslant C{(\phi^{1/2}+\phi^{-1/2})}\eta^{-1}
. Moreover, (
A.9
) directly follows from (
A.2
) and (
A.6
).
In order to prove (
A.10
), we use (
A.2
) to write

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{1}}\left|G_{\mathbf{v}i}\right|^{2}=\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{1}}\left|\left(\Sigma XGX^{*}\Sigma\right)_{\mathbf{v}i}-\Sigma_{\mathbf{v}i}\right|^{2}\leqslant 2\left(\Sigma XGX^{*}\Sigma^{2}XG^{*}X^{*}\Sigma\right)_{\mathbf{vv}}+2\left(\Sigma^{2}\right)_{\mathbf{vv}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left\|X^{*}X\right\|\left(\Sigma XG_{N}G_{N}^{*}X^{*}\Sigma\right)_{\mathbf{vv}}+2\left(\Sigma^{2}\right)_{\mathbf{vv}}=\frac{C\left\|X^{*}X\right\|}{\eta}\operatorname{Im}\left(\Sigma XGX^{*}\Sigma\right)_{\mathbf{vv}}+2\left(\Sigma^{2}\right)_{\mathbf{vv}}

\displaystyle=\frac{C\left\|X^{*}X\right\|}{\eta}\operatorname{Im}G_{\mathbf{vv}}+2\left(\Sigma^{2}\right)_{\mathbf{vv}}.

In order to prove (
A.11
), we use (
A.2
) and (
A.6
) to get

\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{1}}\left|G_{\mathbf{w}i}\right|^{2}=\left(GX^{*}\Sigma^{2}XG^{*}\right)_{\mathbf{ww}}\leqslant C\left(GX^{*}XG^{*}\right)_{\mathbf{ww}}\leqslant C\left\|X^{*}X\right\|\left(G_{2}G_{2}^{*}\right)_{\mathbf{ww}}.

The estimate (
A.12
) can be proved similarly in the sense that

\sum_{\mu\in\mathcal{I}_{N}}\left|G_{\mathbf{v}\mu}\right|^{2}=|z|^{-2}\left(GXX^{*}G^{*}\right)_{\mathrm{vv}}\leqslant C\left\|X^{*}X\right\|\left(G_{1}G_{1}^{*}\right)_{\mathbf{vv}}.

Finally, the same estimates for
G^{(S)}
instead of
G
using minor modifications of the above arguments.
∎

Finally, we provide some large deviation estimates.

Lemma A.3
.

Let
(\xi_{i})
and
(\zeta_{i})
be two independent families satisfying that
\mathbb{E}\xi_{i}=\mathbb{E}\zeta_{i}=0,\ \mathbb{E}|\xi_{i}|^{2}=\mathbb{E}|\zeta_{i}|^{2}=1
,
(\mathbb{E}|\xi_{i}|^{q})^{1/q}\leq C
and
(\mathbb{E}|\zeta_{i}|^{q})^{1/q}\leq C
for all
q\in\mathbb{N}
and some constant
C>0.
Then for deterministic sequences
(a_{ij})
and
(b_{j}),
we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{i}b_{i}\xi_{i}\prec\left(\sum_{i}|b_{i}|^{2}\right)^{1/2},\ \sum_{i,j}a_{ij}\xi_{i}\zeta_{j}\prec\left(\sum_{i,j}|a_{ij}|^{2}\right)^{1/2},\ \sum_{i\neq j}a_{i}a_{j}\xi_{i}\xi_{j}\prec\left(\sum_{i\neq j}|a_{ij}|^{2}\right)^{1/2}.

Proof.

See Lemma 3.1 of
[
14
]
.
∎

A.2
Entrywise local law and proof of (
5.16
), (
5.17
) and (
5.18
)

In this section, we prove the entrywise local law and (
5.16
), (
5.17
) and (
5.18
). More specifically, we will prove the following proposition.

Proposition A.4
.

Suppose the assumptions of Theorem
5.5
hold. Then we have that (1) of Theorem
5.5
holds when
\mathbf{u}_{k}
and
\mathbf{v}_{k}
are the standard basis in
\mathbb{R}^{p}
and
\mathbb{R}^{n},
respectively, and (2) and (3) of Theorem
5.5
hold.

We first prepare some notations. For each
i\in\mathcal{I}_{1},
we define

m_{i}:=\frac{-\sigma_{i}}{1+\phi^{-1/2}m\sigma_{i}}.

(A.13)

Recall (
2.4
). We find that the functions
m
and
m_{i}
satisfy

\frac{1}{m}=-z-{\frac{\phi^{1/2}}{p}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{1}}m_{i},\quad\frac{1}{m_{i}}=-\frac{1}{\sigma_{i}}-{\phi^{-1/2}}m.

Using (
1.2
), (
5.2
) and (
A.13
), we readily obtain that

\left|m_{i}\right|\asymp\sigma_{i}\quad\text{ for }z\in\mathbf{S}\text{ and }i\in\mathcal{I}_{1}\text{. }

(A.14)

For notational simplicity, we extend the definitions of
\sigma_{i}
and
m_{i}
by setting
\sigma_{\mu}:=1
and
m_{\mu}:=m
for
\mu\in\mathcal{I}_{2}
. Recall (
5.10
). We define the blockwise random control parameters as follows

\Lambda_{2}:=\max_{\mu,\nu\in\mathcal{I}_{2}}\frac{\left|G_{\mu\nu}-\delta_{\mu\nu}m_{\mu}\right|}{\sigma_{\mu}\sigma_{\nu}},\quad\Lambda_{o,2}=\max_{\mu\neq\nu\in\mathcal{I}_{2}}\frac{\left|G_{\mu\nu}\right|}{\sigma_{\mu}\sigma_{\nu}},\ \Lambda_{1}=\max_{i,j\in\mathcal{I}_{1}}\frac{\left|G_{ij}-\delta_{ij}m_{i}\right|}{\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}},\quad\Lambda_{o,1}=\max_{i\neq j\in\mathcal{I}_{1}}\frac{\left|G_{ij}\right|}{\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}},

and

\Lambda_{o,12}=\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{1},\mu\in\mathcal{I}_{2}}\frac{\left|G_{i\mu}\right|}{\sigma_{i}\sigma_{\mu}}.

Armed with the above notations, we further denote

\Theta_{1}:=\left|\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{1}}\left(G_{ii}-m_{i}\right)\right|,\quad\Theta_{2}:=\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\mu\in\mathcal{I}_{2}}\left(G_{\mu\mu}-m\right)\right|=\left|m_{2n}-m\right|.

Moreover, we define

\Lambda:=\phi^{1/2}\Lambda_{1}+\phi^{1/4}\Lambda_{o,12}+\Lambda_{2},\ \Lambda_{o}:=\phi^{1/2}\Lambda_{o,1}+\phi^{1/4}\Lambda_{o,12}+\Lambda_{o,2},\ \Theta:=\phi^{1/2}\Theta_{1}+\Theta_{2}.

(A.15)

Note that all the above parameters depend on
z
implicitly and we have the trivial bound
\Theta=\mathrm{O}\left(\Lambda\right).
With (
A.15
), we further denote the parameters

\Psi_{\Theta,2}:=\sqrt{\frac{\operatorname{Im}m+\Theta}{n\eta}},\ \Psi_{\Theta,1}:=\sqrt{\frac{\operatorname{Im}m+\Theta}{p\eta}},\ \Psi_{\Theta,12}:=\sqrt{\frac{\operatorname{Im}m+\Theta}{\sqrt{pn}\eta}},

and the
z
-dependent probability event
\Xi\equiv\Xi(z)

\Xi=\left\{\Lambda(z)\leqslant(\log n)^{-1}\right\}.

(A.16)

To prove Proposition
A.4
, we basically follow the approach of
[
35
, Section 5]
or Section A.2 of
[
25
]
and focus on the details that departs significantly from that of
[
25
,
35
]
.

A.2.1
Weak entrywise local law

In this section, we prove a weaker version of entrywise local law. It is an analog of
[
35
, Proposition 5.1]
or
[
25
, Lemma A.12]
.

Proposition A.5
.

Suppose that the assumptions of Theorem
5.5
hold. Then
\Lambda\prec(n\eta)^{-1/4}
uniformly in
z\in\mathbf{S}
.

The rest of this subsection is devoted to the proof of Proposition
A.5
. For
G
in (
5.10
), recall that we set
H=G^{-1}.
For
s\in\mathcal{I},
we introduce the conditional expectation

\mathbb{E}_{s}[\cdot]:=\mathbb{E}\left[\cdot|H^{(s)}\right].

Recall (
A.1
). According to Lemma
A.1
, we have that for
i\in\mathcal{I}_{1}

\frac{1}{G_{ii}}=-\frac{1}{\sigma_{i}}-{\frac{\phi^{-1/2}}{n}}\operatorname{Tr}G_{2}^{(i)}-Z_{i},\quad Z_{i}:=\left(1-\mathbb{E}_{i}\right)\left(XG^{(i)}X^{*}\right)_{ii},

(A.17)

and for
\mu\in\mathcal{I}_{2}

\frac{1}{G_{\mu\mu}}=-z-{\frac{\phi^{1/2}}{p}}\operatorname{Tr}G_{1}^{(\mu)}-Z_{\mu},\quad Z_{\mu}:=\left(1-\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\right)\left(X^{*}G^{(\mu)}X\right)_{\mu\mu}.

(A.18)

In order to prove Proposition
A.5
, we start with the following estimate which is analogous to Lemma 5.2 of
[
35
]
or
[
25
, Lemma A.9]
. Recall (
A.16
).

Lemma A.6
.

Suppose the assumptions of Proposition
A.5
hold. Then for
s\in\mathcal{I}
and
z\in\mathbf{S},
we have

\mathbf{1}(\Xi)\left(\left|Z_{s}\right|+\Lambda_{o}\right)\prec\Psi_{\Theta,2},

(A.19)

and

\mathbf{1}(\eta\geqslant 1)\left(\left|Z_{s}\right|+\Lambda_{o}\right)\prec\Psi_{\Theta,2}.

(A.20)

Proof.

Due to similarity, we mainly focus on the proof of (
A.19
). Using (
A.14
), (
A.5
) and a simple induction argument, it is not hard to see that

\mathbf{1}(\Xi)\left|G_{tt}^{(S)}\right|\asymp\sigma_{t},

(A.21)

for any
S\subset\mathcal{I}
and
t\in\mathcal{I}\backslash S
satisfying
|S|\leqslant C
. We first work with
\Lambda_{o}
in (
A.15
). It suffices to prove that

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}(\Xi)\left|G_{ij}\right|\prec\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}\left(\frac{\operatorname{Im}m+\Theta_{2}+\Lambda_{o,12}^{2}}{p\eta}\right)^{1/2},

(A.22)

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}(\Xi)\left|G_{\mu\nu}\right|\prec\sigma_{\mu}\sigma_{\nu}\left(\frac{\operatorname{Im}m+\sqrt{\phi}\Theta_{1}+\Lambda_{o,12}^{2}}{n\eta}\right)^{1/2},

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}(\Xi)\left|G_{i\mu}\right|\prec\sigma_{i}\sigma_{\mu}\left(\frac{\operatorname{Im}m+\Theta_{2}+\Lambda_{o,2}^{2}}{\sqrt{pn}\eta}\right)^{1/2}.

In fact, using the second estimate in (
A.22
), it is not hard to see that on
\Xi

\Lambda_{o,2}\prec\Psi_{\Theta,2}+(n\eta)^{-1/2}\Lambda_{o,12}.

Moreover, using the first estimate in (
A.22
), we see that on
\Xi

\phi^{1/2}\Lambda_{o,1}\prec\Psi_{\Theta,2}+(n\eta)^{-1/2}\Lambda_{o,12}.

Similarly, using the last estimate in (
A.22
), we have that on
\Xi

\phi^{1/4}\Lambda_{o,12}\prec\Psi_{\Theta,2}+(n\eta)^{-1/2}\Lambda_{o,2}.

Combining the above bounds with the definition
\Lambda_{o}
in (
A.15
), we see that

\mathbf{1}(\Xi)\Lambda_{o}\prec\Psi_{\Theta,2}.
Now we prove (
A.22
). Let us start with
G_{ij}
for
i\neq j\in\mathcal{I}_{1}
. By (
A.4
), (
A.21
) and Lemma
A.3
, we find on
\Xi

\displaystyle\left|G_{ij}\right|

\displaystyle\leqslant\left|G_{ii}G_{jj}^{(i)}\right|\left|\sum_{\mu,\nu\in\mathcal{I}_{2}}x_{i\mu}G_{\mu\nu}^{(ij)}x_{\nu j}\right|\prec\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}\left(\frac{1}{np}\sum_{\mu,\nu\in\mathcal{I}_{2}}\left|G_{\mu\nu}^{(ij)}\right|^{2}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}\left(\frac{1}{np\eta}\sum_{\mu\in\mathcal{I}_{2}}\operatorname{Im}G_{\mu\mu}^{(ij)}\right)^{1/2}\prec\left(\frac{1}{np\eta}\sum_{\mu\in\mathcal{I}_{2}}\operatorname{Im}G_{\mu\mu}+\frac{\Lambda_{o,12}^{2}}{p\eta}\right)^{1/2}\prec\sqrt{\frac{\operatorname{Im}m+\Theta_{2}+\Lambda_{o,12}^{2}}{p\eta}},

where in the third step we used (
A.9
) and the fourth step we used (
A.5
). This completes the proof for the first estimate of (
A.22
). Similarly, for

G_{\mu\nu}
with
\mu\neq\nu\in\mathcal{I}_{2},
using (1) of Lemma
A.1
and (
A.10
), as well as the bound
\operatorname{Im}m_{i}=\mathrm{O}({\phi^{-1/2}}\sigma_{i}^{2}\operatorname{Im}m)
for all
i\in\mathcal{I}_{1}
which follows from (
A.13
), we have that on
\Xi

\displaystyle\left|G_{\mu\nu}\right|

\displaystyle\leqslant\left|G_{\mu\mu}G_{\nu\nu}^{(\mu)}\right|\left|\sum_{i,j\in\mathcal{I}_{1}}x_{\mu i}G_{ij}^{(\mu\nu)}x_{j\nu}\right|\prec\sigma_{\mu}\sigma_{\nu}\left(\frac{1}{np}\sum_{i,j\in\mathcal{I}_{1}}\left|G_{ij}^{(\mu\nu)}\right|^{2}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\prec\left(\frac{1}{np}\frac{\phi^{1/2}}{\eta}\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}_{1}}\operatorname{Im}G_{kk}^{(\mu\nu)}+\frac{1}{np}\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}_{1}}((\Sigma^{(\mu\nu)})^{2})_{kk}\right)^{1/2}\prec\sqrt{\frac{\operatorname{Im}m+\phi^{1/2}\Theta_{1}+\phi^{1/2}\Lambda_{o,12}^{2}}{n\eta}}.

This completes the proof for the second estimate of (
A.22
). The last estimate on
\mathbf{1}(\Xi)G_{i\mu}
with
i\in\mathcal{I}_{1}
and
\mu\in\mathcal{I}_{N}
can be estimated similarly using (3) of Lemma
A.1
and (
A.11
) in the sense that

\displaystyle\left|G_{i\mu}\right|

\displaystyle=|G_{ii}G_{\mu\mu}^{(i)}|\left|-x_{i\mu}+\left(XG^{(i\mu)}X\right)_{i\mu}\right|\prec\left(\frac{1}{np}\sum_{\nu\in\mathcal{I}_{2},j\in\mathcal{I}_{1}}\left|G_{\nu j}^{(i\mu)}\right|^{2}\right)^{1/2}.

\displaystyle\prec

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\phi^{1/2}}{np}\sum_{\nu\in\mathcal{I}_{2}}\frac{\operatorname{Im}G_{\nu\nu}^{(i\mu)}}{\eta}\right)^{1/2}\prec{\frac{\phi^{1/2}\operatorname{Im}m+\phi^{1/2}\Theta_{2}+\phi^{1/2}\Lambda_{o,2}^{2}}{p\eta}}=\frac{\operatorname{Im}m+\Theta_{2}+\Lambda_{o,2}^{2}}{\sqrt{np}\eta}.

This completes the proof of (
A.22
).

Then we proceed to the control of
Z_{s}.
On the one hand, when
s=i\in\mathcal{I}_{1},
we decompose that

\left|Z_{i}\right|\leqslant\left|\sum_{\mu\in\mathcal{I}_{2}}^{(i)}\left(x_{i\mu}^{2}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{np}}\right)G_{\mu\mu}^{(i)}\right|+\left|\sum_{\mu\neq\nu}^{(i)}x_{i\mu}G_{\mu\nu}^{(i)}x_{\nu i}\right|.

By Lemma
A.3
and (
A.9
), on
\Xi,
we have that the first term of the above equation can be stochastically dominated by
\left(\sum_{\mu}^{(i)}(pn)^{-1}\left|G_{\mu\mu}^{(i)}\right|^{2}\right)^{1/2}\prec\frac{1}{\sqrt{p\eta}}
. For the second term, we have

\sum_{\mu,\nu}^{(i)}\frac{1}{np}\left|G_{\mu\nu}^{(i)}\right|^{2}\leqslant\frac{1}{np}\sum_{\mu,\nu}^{(i)}\left|G_{\mu\nu}^{(i)}\right|^{2}=\frac{1}{np\eta}\sum_{\mu}^{(i)}\operatorname{Im}G_{\mu\mu}^{(i)}\prec\frac{\operatorname{Im}m+\Theta_{2}+\Lambda_{o}^{2}}{p\eta}.

Since
p\gg n,
this results in

\left|Z_{i}\right|\prec\sqrt{\frac{\operatorname{Im}m+\Theta+\Lambda_{o}^{2}}{p\eta}}\prec\Psi_{\Theta,2}.

Similarly, for
s=\mu\in\mathcal{I}_{2},
we can show that

|Z_{\mu}|\prec\sqrt{\frac{\operatorname{Im}m+\phi^{1/2}\Theta_{1}+\Lambda_{o}^{2}}{n\eta}}\prec\Psi_{\Theta,2}.

This completes the proof of (
A.19
).

Finally, for the proof of (
A.20
), the argument is similar except we need to use the bound
\|G^{(S)}\|=\mathrm{O}(1)
which follows from Lemma
A.2
and
\eta\geq 1
whenever it is necessary instead of (
A.21
). This completes our proof.

∎

The following estimate is an analog of
[
25
, Lemma A.10]
or
[
35
, Lemma 5.3]
.

Lemma A.7
.

Suppose the assumptions of Proposition
A.5
hold.
Denote

[Z]_{2}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\mu\in\mathcal{I}_{2}}Z_{\mu},\quad[Z]_{1}:=\frac{\phi^{1/2}}{p}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{1}}\frac{\sigma_{i}^{2}}{\left(1+\phi^{-1/2}m_{2n}\sigma_{i}\right)^{2}}Z_{i}.

(A.23)

Then for
z\in\mathbf{S},
we have for
f
defined in (
2.7
)

\mathbf{1}(\Xi)\left(f\left(m_{2n}\right)-z\right)=\mathbf{1}(\Xi)\left([Z]_{2}+[Z]_{1}+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(\Psi_{\Theta,2}^{2}\right)\right),

(A.24)

and

\mathbf{1}(\eta\geq 1)\left(f\left(m_{2n}\right)-z\right)=\mathbf{1}(\eta\geq 1)\left([Z]_{2}+[Z]_{1}+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(n^{-1}\right)\right).

(A.25)

Proof.

As before, due to similarity, we focus our proof on (
A.24
). Recall that
m_{2n}=n^{-1}\sum_{\mu\in\mathcal{I}_{2}}G_{\mu\mu}(z).
Using
G_{\mu\mu}=m_{2n}+\left(G_{\mu\mu}-m_{2n}\right),
we see that

\frac{1}{G_{\mu\mu}}=\frac{1}{m_{2n}}-\frac{1}{m_{2n}^{2}}\left(G_{\mu\mu}-m_{2n}\right)+\frac{1}{m_{2n}^{2}}\left(G_{\mu\mu}-m_{2n}\right)^{2}\frac{1}{G_{\mu\mu}}.

(A.26)

Moreover, according to (
A.18
), using Lemma
A.6
and (
A.5
), we see that

\mathbf{1}(\Xi)\frac{1}{G_{\mu\mu}}=\mathbf{1}(\Xi)\left(-z-\frac{\phi^{1/2}}{p}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{1}}G_{ii}-Z_{\mu}+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left({\phi^{1/2}}\Psi_{\Theta,{12}}^{2}\right)\right),

(A.27)

and

\mathbf{1}(\Xi)\left|G_{\mu\mu}-m_{2n}\right|\prec\Psi_{\Theta,2}.

(A.28)

Similarly, according to (
A.17
), we have that

\mathbf{1}(\Xi)G_{ii}=\mathbf{1}(\Xi)\frac{-\sigma_{i}}{1+\phi^{-1/2}m_{2n}\sigma_{i}+\sigma_{i}Z_{i}+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(\sigma_{i}\phi^{-1/2}\Psi_{\Theta,{12}}^{2}\right)}.

(A.29)

On the one hand, combining (
A.26
) and (
A.28
), we see that

\mathbf{1}(\Xi)\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\mu\in\mathcal{I}_{2}}\frac{1}{G_{\mu\mu}}=\mathbf{1}(\Xi)\frac{1}{m_{2n}}+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(\Psi_{\Theta,2}^{2}\right).

(A.30)

On the other hand, plugging (
A.29
) into (
A.27
), we see that

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}(\Xi)\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\mu\in\mathcal{I}_{2}}\frac{1}{G_{\mu\mu}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}(\Xi)\left(-z+\frac{\phi^{1/2}}{p}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{1}}\frac{\sigma_{i}}{1+\phi^{-1/2}m_{2n}\sigma_{i}+\sigma_{i}Z_{i}+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(\sigma_{i}\phi^{1/2}\Psi_{\Theta,12}^{2}\right)}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\mu\in\mathcal{I}_{2}}Z_{\mu}+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(\Psi_{\Theta,2}^{2}\right)\right).

Moreover, using (
A.21
) and the assumption
p\gg n
, we see that
1+\phi^{-1/2}m_{2n}\sigma_{i}\asymp 1.

Therefore, together with (
A.30
), we can conclude the proof of (
A.24
).

The proof of (
A.25
) is similar as discussed in the end of the proof of Lemma
A.6
. We omit further details.

∎

The following lemma provides the stability condition for (
2.4
). Roughly, it says that if
f(m_{2n})-z
is small and
m_{2n}(\tilde{z})-m(\tilde{z})
is small for
\tilde{z}:=z+\mathrm{i}{n^{-5}}
, then
m_{2n}(z)-m(z)
is small. For
z\in\mathbf{S},
we introduce the discrete set

L(z):=\{z\}\cup\left\{w\in\mathbf{S}:\operatorname{Re}w=\operatorname{Re}z,\operatorname{Im}w\in[\operatorname{Im}z,1]\cap\left(n^{-5}{\phi^{-1/2}}\mathbb{N}\right)\right\}.

Thus, if
\operatorname{Im}z\geqslant 1
then
L(z)=\{z\}
and if
\operatorname{Im}z\leqslant 1
then
L(z)
is a one-dimensional lattice with spacing
n^{-5}{\phi^{-1/2}}
plus the point
z
. Clearly, we have the bound
|L(z)|\leqslant n^{5}{\phi^{1/2}}
.

Lemma A.8
.

Suppose that
\delta:\mathbf{S}\rightarrow(0,\infty)
satisfies
n^{-2}\leqslant\delta(z)\leqslant(\log n)^{-1}
for
z\in\mathbf{S}
and that
\delta
is Lipschitz continuous with Lipschitz constant
n^{2}{\phi^{1/2}}
. Suppose moreover that for each fixed
E
, the function
\eta\mapsto\delta(E+\mathrm{i}\eta)
is nonincreasing for
\eta>0
. Suppose that
u:\mathbf{S}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
is the Stieltjes transform of a probability measure supported in
[0,C]
for some large enough constant
C>0
. Let
w\in L(z)
and suppose that

|f(u(w))-w|\leqslant\delta(w).

Then we have that

|u(z)-m(z)|\leqslant\frac{C^{\prime}\delta(z)}{\operatorname{Im}m(z)+\sqrt{\delta(z)}},

(A.31)

for some constant
C^{\prime}>0
independent of
z
and
n.

Proof.

This lemma can proved with the same method as in e.g.
[
14
, Lemma 4.5]
and
[
35
, Appendix A.2]
. The main inputs are Lemmas
2.5
and
5.1
and some estimates in their proofs. We omit further details.
∎

Armed with the above results, we can follow
[
14
,
25
,
35
]
to complete the proof.

Proof of Proposition
A.5
.

When
\eta\geq 1,
using the above controls and Lemma
5.1
, following the same arguments as in
[
14
, Lemma 4.6]
or
[
25
, (A.44)-(A.46)]
, we find that
\Lambda\prec n^{-1/4}.
It remains to deal with the small
\eta
case. One can prove this lemma using a continuity argument as in e.g.
[
14
, Section 4.1]
. The key inputs are Lemmas
A.6
–
A.8
, and the estimates in the
\eta\geq 1
case. All
the other parts of the proof are essentially the same. We omit the details.
∎

A.2.2
Proof of Proposition
A.4

To get strong laws as in Proposition
A.4
, we need stronger bounds on
[Z]_{2}
and
[Z]_{1}
in (
A.23
). The following lemma is a counterpart of Lemma A.13 of
[
25
]
or Lemma 5.6 of
[
35
]
or Lemma 4.9 of
[
14
]
. Due to similarity, we only sketch the proof.

Lemma A.9
.

Suppose the assumptions of Proposition
A.4
hold. Moreover, if
\Upsilon
is a positive,
n
-dependent, deterministic function on
\mathbf{S}
satisfying
n^{-1/2}\leqslant\Upsilon\leqslant n^{-c}
for some constant
c>0
. In addition, if we assume that that
\Lambda_{2}\prec n^{-c}
and
\Lambda_{o,2}\prec\Upsilon
on
\mathbf{S}
. Then on
\mathbf{S},
we have

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\mu\in\mathcal{I}_{2}}\left(1-\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\right)\frac{1}{G_{\mu\mu}}=\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(\Upsilon^{2}\right).

Similarly, for some other positive deterministic function on
\mathbf{S}
satisfying
p^{-1/2}\leqslant\widetilde{\Upsilon}\leqslant p^{-c}
, suppose that
\Lambda_{1}\prec p^{-c}
and
\Lambda_{o,1}\prec\widetilde{\Upsilon}
on
\mathbf{S}
. Then we have

\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{1}}\frac{\sigma_{i}^{2}}{\left(1+\phi^{-1/2}m_{2n}\sigma_{i}\right)^{2}}\left(1-\mathbb{E}_{i}\right)\frac{1}{G_{ii}}=\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{2}\right).

Proof.

As discussed in the proof of Lemma 5.6 of
[
35
]
, the proof of the first estimate follows from a straightforward extension of Lemma 4.9 of
[
14
]
. For the second estimate, it can be proved similarly using the discussions as explained in Appendix B of
[
14
]
. The only complication is the term

\frac{\sigma_{i}^{2}}{\left(1+\phi^{-1/2}m_{2n}\sigma_{i}\right)^{2}}=\frac{\sigma_{i}^{2}}{\left(1+\phi^{-1/2}m\sigma_{i}\right)^{2}}-\frac{\sigma_{i}^{2}[\phi^{-1}(m_{2n}^{2}-m^{2})\sigma_{i}^{2}+2\phi^{-1/2}(m_{2n}-m)\sigma_{i}]}{(1+\phi^{-1/2}m_{2n}\sigma_{i})^{2}(1+\phi^{-1/2}m\sigma_{i})^{2}}.

On the one hand, running the argument on
\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{1}}\frac{\sigma_{i}^{2}}{\left(1+\phi^{-1/2}m\sigma_{i}\right)^{2}}\left(1-\mathbb{E}_{i}\right)\frac{1}{G_{ii}}=\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{2}\right)
is a straightforward extension of the proof of the first estimate. On the other hand, for the second term, since we have
|m_{2n}-m|\prec(n\eta)^{-1/4}
from Proposition
A.5
, we see that

\frac{\sigma_{i}^{2}[\phi^{-1}(m_{2n}^{2}-m^{2})\sigma_{i}^{2}+2\phi^{-1/2}(m_{2n}-m)\sigma_{i}]}{(1+\phi^{-1/2}m_{2n}\sigma_{i})^{2}(1+\phi^{-1/2}m\sigma_{i})^{2}}\prec\phi^{-1/2}(n\eta)^{-1/4}.

Using Proposition
A.5
, we see that

\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{1}}\frac{\sigma_{i}^{2}[\phi^{-1}(m_{2n}^{2}-m^{2})\sigma_{i}^{2}+2\phi^{-1/2}(m_{2n}-m)\sigma_{i}]}{(1+\phi^{-1/2}m_{2n}\sigma_{i})^{2}(1+\phi^{-1/2}m\sigma_{i})^{2}}(1-\mathbb{E}_{i})\frac{1}{G_{ii}}\prec\phi^{-1/2}(n\eta)^{-1/2}.

This completes the proof. For more details, we refer the readers to
[
14
, Appendix B]
,
[
35
, Lemma 5.6]
and
[
25
, Lemma A.13]
.
∎

Armed with Lemma
A.9
, we can prove the following iterative estimates which is an analog of Lemma 4.8 of
[
14
]
.

Lemma A.10
.

Let
t\in(0,1)
and suppose that
\Theta\prec(n\eta)^{-t}

uniformly in
z\in\mathbf{S}
. Then we have that uniformly in
z\in\mathbf{S}

|[Z]_{1}|+|[Z]_{2}|\prec\frac{\operatorname{Im}m+(n\eta)^{-t}}{n\eta}.

Proof.

Due to similarity, we focus on
[Z]_{2}.
According to (
A.18
) and (1) of Lemma
A.1
, we see that

-Z_{u}=(1-\mathbb{E}_{\mu})\frac{1}{G_{\mu\mu}}.

(A.32)

We now apply Lemma
A.9
to the above identity. According to Proposition
A.5
and its proof, we conclude that

\Lambda_{o,2}\prec\Upsilon,\quad\Lambda\prec\Upsilon^{\prime},\quad\Upsilon:=\sqrt{\frac{\operatorname{Im}m+(n\eta)^{-t}}{n\eta}},\quad\Upsilon^{\prime}:=(n\eta)^{-1/4}.

Using Lemma
5.1
, it is easy to see that the conditions of
\Upsilon
and
\Upsilon^{\prime}
satisfy the conditions of Lemma
A.9
so that the result follows from Lemma
A.9
and (
A.32
). Similar discussions apply to
[Z]_{1}
using

\widetilde{\Upsilon}:=\sqrt{\frac{\operatorname{Im}m+(n\eta)^{-t}}{p\eta}},\quad\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{\prime}=(p\eta)^{-1/4}.

This completes the proof.
∎

Armed with Lemma
A.10
and Proposition
A.5
, we can prove Proposition
A.4
following the arguments in
[
14
, Section 4.2]
verbatim. Due to similarity, we only sketch the proof.

Proof of Proposition
A.4
.

We notice that Lemma
A.10
implies that if
\Theta\prec(n\eta)^{-\tau},
one may invoke
(
A.31
) to obtain that for some constant
C>0

\Theta_{2}\prec\frac{\operatorname{Im}m}{n\eta}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\kappa+\eta}}+\sqrt{\frac{(n\eta)^{-\tau}}{n\eta}}\leqslant C(n\eta)^{-1/2-\tau/2}.

According to Proposition
A.5
, we can iterate the above results and push the rate all the way down to

\Theta_{2}\prec(n\eta)^{-1}
on
\mathbf{S}
. Similarly, we can obtain the control for
\phi^{1/2}\Theta_{1}
and show that
\Theta\prec(n\eta)^{-1}
using the definition (
A.15
). This completes the proof of (
5.16
) and (
5.17
). Once the averaged local law is proved, we can prove the entrywise local law (
5.18
) and (
5.14
) and (
5.15
) with standard basis using Proposition
A.5
and Lemma
A.1
; see Section Section 4.2 of
[
14
]
for more details. This concludes our proof.
∎

A.3
Proof of (
5.14
) and (
5.15
)

It remains to prove (
5.14
) and (
5.15
) for general projections
\mathbf{u}
and
\mathbf{v}.
Our argument basically follows that of Section 5 of
[
14
]
. We only sketch the proof and refer the readers to
[
14
]
for more details. Due to similarity, we only provide the arguments for the proof of (
5.14
). By linearity and polarization, we see that it suffices to prove that for any fix deterministic unit vector
\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{p},

\mathbf{v}^{*}R_{1}\mathbf{v}=\mathbf{v}^{*}\Pi\mathbf{v}+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}(\phi^{-1}\Psi).
Furthermore, using the definitions in (
5.11
), (
5.12
), (
A.1
) and (
A.13
), it suffices to prove

\mathbf{v}^{*}G_{1}(z)\mathbf{v}=\mathbf{v}^{*}M\mathbf{v}+\mathrm{O}_{\prec}\left(\phi^{-1/2}\Psi\right),

(A.33)

where for
m_{i}
in (
A.13
) we denote

M=\operatorname{diag}\{m_{1},m_{2},\cdots,m_{p}\}.

The rest of this section is devoted to the proof of (
A.33
). Throughout the proof of this section, to make more explicit connections with Section 5 of
[
14
]
, with a little abuse of notations, for (
A.1
), we use

G\equiv G_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times p},\ R\equiv G_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}.

(A.34)

Moreover, we will use
a,b
for indices valued in
\{1,2,\ldots,p\}
and
\mathbf{v}=(v_{a}).
We decompose that

\mathbf{v}^{*}G\mathbf{v}-\mathbf{v}^{*}M\mathbf{v}=\sum_{a}v_{a}^{2}(G_{aa}-m_{i})+\mathcal{Z},

where

\mathcal{Z}:=\sum_{a\neq b}v_{a}G_{ab}v_{b}.

(A.35)

Note that the first part can be can be controlled using Proposition
A.4
in the following way

|\sum_{a}v_{a}^{2}(G_{aa}-m_{a})|\leq\max_{a}|G_{aa}-m_{a}|\prec\phi^{-1/2}\Psi.

(A.36)

It suffices to control
\mathcal{Z}.
Throughout this section, we will follow Section 5 of
[
14
]
to prove that the following result. Note that due to our linearization as in (
A.1
) and (
A.2
), we do not need to rescale the resolvent as in Section 5.1 of
[
14
]
.

Lemma A.11
.

Suppose the assumptions of Theorem
5.5
hold. Then we have that for all
q\in\mathbb{N},

\mathbb{E}|\mathcal{Z}|^{q}\prec\left(\phi^{-1/2}\Psi\right)^{q}.

Proof.

The proof is almost the same as Sections 5.5-5.15 of
[
14
]
. We omit the details.
∎

Armed with Lemma
A.11
, we see from Chebyshev’s inequality that
\mathcal{Z}\prec\phi^{-1/2}\Psi.
Combining with (
A.36
), we can complete the proof of (
A.33
) and hence (
5.14
).

Appendix B
Additional technical proofs

In this section, we prove Lemmas
2.5
and
5.1
.

B.1
Proof of (
5.2
)

Taking imaginary part and real part on both sides of (
2.4
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{\operatorname{Re}m}{|m|^{2}}=-E+\int\frac{\phi(\phi^{1/2}x^{-1}+\operatorname{Re}m)}{|\phi^{1/2}x^{-1}+m|^{2}}\pi(\mathrm{d}x),

\displaystyle\frac{-\operatorname{Im}m}{|m|^{2}}=-\eta+\int\frac{\phi(-\operatorname{Im}m)}{|\phi^{1/2}x^{-1}+m|^{2}}\pi(\mathrm{d}x).

(B.1)

For simplicity, we denote
R:=\operatorname{Re}m
and
I:=\operatorname{Im}m
. We can further rewrite the above equations as

\displaystyle R\int\frac{(\phi^{1/2}x^{-1}+R)^{2}+I^{2}-\phi(R^{2}+I^{2})}{(R^{2}+I^{2})[(\phi^{1/2}x^{-1}+R)^{2}+I^{2}]}\pi(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle=-E+\int\frac{\phi^{3/2}x^{-1}}{(\phi^{1/2}x^{-1}+R)^{2}+I^{2}}\pi(\mathrm{d}x),

(B.2)

\displaystyle I\int\frac{(\phi^{1/2}x^{-1}+R)^{2}+I^{2}-\phi(R^{2}+I^{2})}{(R^{2}+I^{2})[(\phi^{1/2}x^{-1}+R)^{2}+I^{2}]}\pi(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle=\eta.

Recall that the Stieltjes transform should map
\mathbb{C}_{+}
to
\mathbb{C}_{+}.
Therefore, for all
z\in\mathbb{C}_{+},
we see from the second equation of (
B.2
) that

\int\frac{(\phi^{1/2}x^{-1}+R)^{2}+I^{2}-\phi(R^{2}+I^{2})}{(R^{2}+I^{2})[(\phi^{1/2}x^{-1}+R)^{2}+I^{2}]}\pi(\mathrm{d}x)>0.

This implies that there must exist
x_{0}\in\operatorname{supp}(\pi)
so that

(\phi^{1/2}x_{0}^{-1}+R)^{2}+I^{2}-\phi(R^{2}+I^{2})>0.

Set
t:=|m|=\sqrt{R^{2}+I^{2}}
. We see that for all
\phi>0

\displaystyle(\phi-1)t^{2}-2\phi^{1/2}x_{0}^{-1}t-\phi x_{0}^{-2}

\displaystyle<0.

Consequently, for
\phi\gg 1,
we shall have that
\forall z\in\mathbb{C}_{+}

t<\frac{2\phi^{1/2}x_{0}^{-1}+2\sqrt{\phi x_{0}^{-2}+\phi(\phi-1)x_{0}^{-2}}}{2(\phi-1)}\sim x_{0}^{-1}(1+\phi^{-1/2})=\mathrm{O}(1).

This proves the upper bound for
|m|.
For the lower bound, equipped with the upper bound, according to (2) of Assumption
2.1
, it is easy to see from (
B.2
) that when
\phi\gg 1

\displaystyle R\left(\frac{1}{R^{2}+I^{2}}-\int x^{2}\pi(\mathrm{d}x)+\mathrm{O}(\phi^{-1/2})\right)

\displaystyle=-E+\int\frac{\phi^{3/2}x^{-1}}{(\phi^{1/2}x^{-1}+R)^{2}+I^{2}}\pi(\mathrm{d}x),

\displaystyle I\left(\frac{1}{R^{2}+I^{2}}-\int x^{2}\pi(\mathrm{d}x)+\mathrm{O}(\phi^{-1/2})\right)

\displaystyle=\eta.

This yields that

\displaystyle(R^{2}+I^{2})\left(\frac{1}{R^{2}+I^{2}}-\int x^{2}\pi(\mathrm{d}x)+\mathrm{O}(\phi^{-1/2})\right)^{2}=\left(-E+\int\frac{\phi^{3/2}x^{-1}}{(\phi^{1/2}x^{-1}+R)^{2}+I^{2}}\pi(\mathrm{d}x)\right)^{2}+\eta^{2}.

Using the upper bound for
t,
when
z\in\mathbf{S}
in (
5.1
), we see that the right-hand side of the above equation can be bounded by
\mathrm{O}(1).
Moreover, the left-hand side of the above equation can be rewritten as
\frac{(t^{-2}-\int x^{2}\pi(\mathrm{d}x))^{2}}{t^{-2}}.
It shows that
t
is bounded from both above and below so that
m(z)\asymp 1
. Together with (
B.1
), we see that
\operatorname{Im}m
is bounded from below by
c\eta.
This concludes the proof.

B.2
Proof of Lemma
2.5
and (
5.3
)

The proofs are similar to those in
[
35
]
. Recall
f
defined in (
2.7
) and the intervals
I_{k},0\leq k\leq 2p,
defined below it. We abbreviate
r_{i}:=\phi\pi(\{s_{i}\})
. Then we can rewrite

f(x)=-\frac{1}{x}+\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{r_{i}}{x+s_{i}^{-1}}.

By definition of
f
we have

f^{\prime}(x)=\frac{1}{x^{2}}-\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{r_{i}}{\left(x+s_{i}^{-1}\right)^{2}},\quad f^{\prime\prime}(x)=\frac{-2}{x^{3}}+2\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{r_{i}}{\left(x+s_{i}^{-1}\right)^{3}}.

(B.3)

Denote the multiset
\mathcal{C}\subset\mathbb{R}
of the critical points of
f,
using the conventions that a nondegenerate
critical point is counted once and a degenerate critical point twice. According to Lemma 2.4 of
[
35
]
, we have that
|\mathcal{C}\cap I_{0}|=|\mathcal{C}\cap I_{1}|=1
and
|\mathcal{C}\cap I_{i}|\in\{0,2\}
for
i=2,\cdots,p.
This implies that
|\mathcal{C}|=2q
is even and we denote by
x_{1}\geq x_{2}\geq\cdots\geq x_{2q-1}\geq x_{2q}
be these critical points. Moreover, when
\phi\gg 1,
we find that for
0<t\leq 1,

\partial_{t}\partial_{x}f^{t}(x)=-\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\phi t\pi(\{\sigma_{i}\})x}{(x+(\phi t)^{1/2}\sigma_{i}^{-1})^{3}}<0,\ x\in I_{2}\cup I_{3}\cup\cdots\cup I_{p}.

Following lines of the proof of Lemma 2.5 of
[
35
]
, we have that for
a_{k}=f(x_{k}),

a_{1}\geq a_{2}\geq\cdots\geq a_{2q}
and
x_{k}=m(a_{k}).

Note that by definition,
x_{k}\asymp\phi^{1/2}
for
k\in\{2,\cdots,2q-1\}.
However, according to (
5.2
) that
m(z)
is bounded above and the relation that
f(m(z))=z
, this shows that
q=1
and we only have two critical points in
I_{1}
and
I_{0},
denoted as
x_{1}
and
x_{2}.
Following lines of the proof of Lemma 2.6 of
[
35
]
, we can complete the first part of Lemma
2.5
. To prove the second part of Lemma
2.5
and (
5.3
), we need the following lemma which is an analog of Lemma A.3 of
[
35
]
.

Lemma B.1
.

There exists a
\tau>0,
such that for
k=1,2
and some constant
C>0

\left|x_{k}\right|\asymp 1,\quad\left|f^{\prime\prime}\left(x_{k}\right)\right|\asymp 1,\quad\left|f^{\prime\prime\prime}(\zeta)\right|\leqslant C\quad\text{ for }\quad\left|\zeta-x_{k}\right|\leqslant\tau^{\prime}.

Proof.

First, the above bounds for
x_{k}
are clear. The lower bound
\left|x_{k}\right|\geqslant{c}
follows from
f^{\prime}\left(x_{k}\right)=0
and (
B.3
). Second, using

f^{\prime\prime}\left(x_{k}\right)=-\frac{2}{x_{k}}\sum_{i}\frac{s_{i}^{-1}r_{i}}{\left(x_{k}+s_{i}^{-1}\right)^{3}},

and
|x_{k}|\asymp 1,
we see that
|f^{\prime\prime}(x_{k})|\asymp 1.
Finally, the last control follows from

f^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)=\frac{6}{x^{4}}-6\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\phi\pi(\{\sigma_{i}\})}{(x+\phi^{1/2}\sigma_{i}^{-1})^{4}}.

∎

Using (
2.7
),
x_{1},x_{2}\asymp 1
and
\gamma_{+}=f(x_{1}),\gamma_{-}=f(x_{2}),
when
\phi\geq 1,
it is easy to see that
\gamma_{+},\gamma_{-}\asymp\phi^{1/2}.
Similarly, we can show that
\gamma_{+}-\gamma_{-}=f(x_{1})-f(x_{2})=\mathrm{O}(1).
This completes the second part of Lemma
2.5
. Finally, (
5.3
) follows from the following expansion

z-a_{k}=\frac{f^{\prime\prime}\left(x_{k}\right)}{2}\left(m(z)-x_{k}\right)^{2}+\mathrm{O}\left(\left|m(z)-x_{k}\right|^{3}\right)\quad\text{ for }\quad\left|z-a_{k}\right|\leqslant\tau.

Due to similarity, we omit more details and refer the readers to Section A.2 of
[
35
]
or Lemma C.5 of
[
26
]
for more details.

Finally, we prove (
5.4
) following that of Lemma 8.5 of
[
43
]
. Using (
5.6
), we obtain that

E\operatorname{Im}m+\eta\operatorname{Re}m=\frac{\phi^{1/2}}{p}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\sigma_{i}\operatorname{Im}m}{|1+\phi^{-1/2}m\sigma_{i}|^{2}},

and

E\operatorname{Re}m-\eta\operatorname{Im}m=\phi-1-\frac{\phi}{p}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{1+\phi^{-1/2}\sigma_{i}\operatorname{Im}m}{|1+\phi^{-1/2}m\sigma_{i}|^{2}}.

This yields that

\left|z-\frac{\phi^{1/2}}{p}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\sigma_{i}}{|1+\phi^{-1/2}m\sigma_{i}|^{2}}\right|=\left|\mathrm{i}\eta-\frac{\operatorname{Re}m}{\operatorname{Im}m}\eta\right|=\frac{|m|\eta}{\operatorname{Im}m}.

Together with (
5.2
) and (
5.3
), we obtain an uppper bound
C\sqrt{\kappa+\eta}
for the above term.
Note that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\sigma_{i}}{|1+\phi^{-1/2}m\sigma_{i}|^{2}}-\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\sigma_{i}}{(1+\phi^{-1/2}m\sigma_{i})^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\left|\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\sigma_{i}[(\operatorname{Im}(1+\phi^{-1/2}m\sigma_{i}))^{2}+\mathrm{i}(\operatorname{Im}(1+\phi^{-1/2}m\sigma_{i}))(\operatorname{Re}(1+\phi^{-1/2}m\sigma_{i}))]}{|1+\phi^{-1/2}m\sigma|^{4}}\right|\leq C\phi^{-1/2}\sqrt{\kappa+\eta}.

Combining the above bounds and (
5.7
), we get the upper bound
C\sqrt{\kappa+\eta}
for
|\frac{m}{m^{\prime}}|
, it remains to derive a lower bound.
When
E\in[\gamma_{-},\gamma_{+}]
, we have

\displaystyle\left|z-\frac{\phi^{1/2}}{p}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\sigma_{i}}{(1+\phi^{-1/2}m\sigma_{i})^{2}}\right|\geq\left|\operatorname{Im}\left(z-\frac{\phi^{1/2}}{p}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\sigma_{i}}{(1+\phi^{-1/2}m\sigma_{i})^{2}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\left|\eta+\frac{2}{p}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\sigma_{i}(1+\phi^{-1/2}\operatorname{Re}m\sigma_{i})\sigma_{i}\operatorname{Im}m}{|1+\phi^{-1/2}m\sigma_{i}|^{4}}\right|\geq\frac{2}{p}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\sigma_{i}^{2}(1+\phi^{-1/2}\operatorname{Re}m\sigma_{i})\operatorname{Im}m}{|1+\phi^{-1/2}m\sigma_{i}|^{4}}\geq C{\sqrt{\kappa+\eta}}.

When
E\notin[\gamma_{-},\gamma_{+}]
, we have

\displaystyle\left|z-\frac{\phi^{1/2}}{p}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\sigma_{i}}{(1+\phi^{-1/2}m\sigma_{i})^{2}}\right|\geq\left||z|-\frac{\phi^{1/2}}{p}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\sigma_{i}}{|1+\phi^{-1/2}m\sigma_{i}|^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\sqrt{E^{2}+\eta^{2}}-E-\eta\frac{\operatorname{Re}m}{\operatorname{Im}m}\right|=\left|\eta\frac{\operatorname{Re}m}{\operatorname{Im}m}-\frac{\eta}{E+\sqrt{E^{2}+\eta^{2}}}\right|\geq C\sqrt{\kappa+\eta}.

This completes our proof.

B.3
Proof of (
7.7
)

Let
z=E+\mathrm{i}\eta
and
E\in\mathbf{R}.
First, when
\eta\geq\eta_{c}:=n^{-1+\tau},
we see that the result follows from Theorem
5.5
. Then we handle the case when
\eta\leq\eta_{c}.
Denote
z_{c}:=E+\mathrm{i}{\eta}_{c}
. Since
\operatorname{Tr}R_{1}(z)=(p-n)/z+\operatorname{Tr}R_{2}(z),
it is equivalent to establishing a bound for
\operatorname{Tr}R_{2}(z)
. In view of (
A.2
), it suffices to study
(1-\mathbb{E})\sum_{\mu\in\mathcal{I}_{2}}G_{\mu\mu}
. Using (
A.6
), Lemma
5.1
and Theorem
5.5
, we have that

\displaystyle\left|{G}_{\mu\mu}(z)-{G}_{\mu\mu}\left(z_{c}\right)\right|\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{{\eta}_{c}\left|\left\langle\mathbf{e}_{\mu},\bm{\zeta}_{j}\right\rangle\right|\left|\left\langle\mathbf{e}_{\mu},\bm{\zeta}_{j}\right\rangle\right|}{\left|\left(\lambda_{j}-E-\mathrm{i}{\eta}\right)\left(\lambda_{j}-E-\mathrm{i}{\eta}_{c}\right)\right|}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\eta}_{c}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{\left|\left\langle\mathbf{e}_{\mu},\bm{\zeta}_{j}\right\rangle\right|^{2}}{\left|\left(\lambda_{j}-E-\mathrm{i}{\eta}\right)\right|^{2}}\right)^{1/2}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{\left|\left\langle\mathbf{e}_{\mu},\bm{\zeta}_{j}\right\rangle\right|^{2}}{\left|\left(\lambda_{j}-E-\mathrm{i}{\eta}_{c}\right)\right|^{2}}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\eta}_{c}\left(\frac{{\eta}_{c}^{2}}{{\eta}^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{\left|\left\langle\mathbf{e}_{\mu},\bm{\zeta}_{j}\right\rangle\right|^{2}}{\left|\left(\lambda_{j}-E-\mathrm{i}{\eta}_{c}\right)\right|^{2}}\right)^{1/2}\sqrt{\frac{\operatorname{Im}\left[G_{\mu\mu}\left(z_{c}\right)\right]}{{\eta}_{c}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{{\eta}_{c}}{{\eta}}\sqrt{\operatorname{Im}\left[G_{\mu\mu}\left(z_{c}\right)\right]\operatorname{Im}\left[G_{\mu\mu}\left(z_{c}\right)\right]}\prec\frac{1}{n{\eta}}.

We readily obtain that

\displaystyle|(1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{1}}(z)|=|(1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}{R_{2}}(z)|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|(1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}({R_{2}}(z)-{R_{2}}(z_{c}))+(1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}({R_{2}}(z_{c}))|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{\mu\in\mathcal{I}_{2}}\left|{G}_{\mu\mu}(z)-{G}_{\mu\mu}\left(z_{c}\right)\right|+|(1-\mathbb{E})\operatorname{Tr}({R_{2}}(z_{c}))|\prec\frac{1}{{\eta_{c}}}+\frac{1}{|\operatorname{Im}(z)|}\prec\frac{1}{|\operatorname{Im}(z)|}.

This completes our proof.
